- Context: exported games shipped every file raw (the 3D template pack was 6.50 MB, mostly cooked textures and scenes that compress well), entries were checked with FNV-1a 64 (detects accidents, not tampering), and nothing let a game tell its own pack from a replaced one.
- Decision: version 2 of the format. Each entry is stored raw or as one zstd frame (Zstandard 1.5.7, BSD-3-Clause, built from source with `ZSTD_DISABLE_ASM` and a private xxHash namespace); the writer keeps an entry raw unless zstd saves at least 3 % (already-compressed Ogg/PNG data costs no decode time). Default level 12 (`--compression-level 1..19`; decode speed does not depend on it). Entries compress on parallel threads with no shared state, and the output stays byte-identical for the same input. Every TOC entry carries the BLAKE2b-256 of its stored bytes; the 64-byte header carries the BLAKE2b-256 of the TOC. An optional trailer holds an Ed25519 (RFC 8032, Monocypher's optional `monocypher-ed25519.c`) public key and a signature over a domain tag plus the header, so one signature covers every byte a reader uses. Readers verify a payload's digest before decompressing it and require a frame to decode to exactly the recorded size, so a hostile pack cannot drive allocations past `max_bytes`. Strict end-of-file checks: nothing may follow the TOC except exactly one signature block. Version 1 packs are still read. `victus pack keygen|verify|list`, `victus export --sign-key FILE --no-compress --compression-level N`; the export records the signer in `export.json`, and the player then refuses an unsigned or differently signed pack (`--pack-key HEX` overrides). OS randomness moved from `engine/net` to `victus::os_random_bytes` in core so both use the same source.
- Threat model (documented in `pack.hpp` and `player/export.hpp`): a pack signed by a trusted key cannot be modified or replaced without the secret key. The player learns which key to trust from `export.json`, which sits next to the pack, so this catches a pack replaced or corrupted on disk, a mixed-up patch or a mod overwriting the game pack, but not an attacker who can also edit `export.json` or the executable; that is the job of platform code signing (still open under B6). Alignment padding between payloads is not authenticated; it is never read.
- Alternatives: whole-archive compression (no random access, every read decompresses from the start); LZ4 (faster decode, about 20-30 % larger on this content); a solid-block format with dictionaries (better ratio on many tiny files, but random access needs block indices; deferred until a project has thousands of small entries); SHA-256 digests (no faster here and Monocypher already ships BLAKE2b); minisign or libsodium (a second crypto library for the same primitives).
- Evidence (Release, GCC 15.2, 2 vCPU, `victus new --template 3d` then `victus export --pack-only`, cooked cache warm, wall time of the CLI): raw 6,500,578 bytes, 0.20 s; zstd 3 2,537,266 bytes, 0.23 s; zstd 12 (default) 2,426,626 bytes (37 %), 0.33 s; zstd 19 2,224,610 bytes, 1.01 s; signed zstd 12 2,426,730 bytes (+104), 0.33 s. `victus pack verify` (open, digest and decode all 21 entries), five runs including process start: raw 0.090 s, zstd 12 0.114 s. All 21 entries of this template compress, including the two 2.9 MB cooked textures (to 38 %). The signed export ran in `victus_player` on lavapipe and rendered the template scene; copying an unsigned pack over it made the player exit with "is not signed, but a signed pack is required".
- Consequences: exported packs are about 2.7x smaller on this template; loading pays zstd decode (hundreds of MB/s per core; not measured on a phone). Packs written by this build cannot be read by older builds (they report a version mismatch). Mobile bundled-pack reinstall detection compares the first 64 bytes, which now end with the TOC digest. Remaining under B6: installers, platform code signing, delta patches, cross-export.

### ADR-0155: Versioned Luau script API with frozen snapshots

- Status: Accepted (2026-11-21). Addresses production blocker B7.
- Context: game code names engine symbols (`scene.find`, `entity.position`, `function M:on_trigger_enter`), and nothing stopped a commit from renaming or removing one. A project had no record of the API its scripts targeted, so an engine upgrade could break a game silently at run time, on the line that used the symbol.
- Decision: the script API gets its own version MAJOR.MINOR (`script::kScriptApiVersion`, `victus/script/api.hpp`), independent of the engine version, with the plugin ABI's rule (ADR-0078): same major and host minor >= required minor. Its surface is the globals VICTUS adds to the sandbox (standard Luau libraries excluded, captured right after `luaL_openlibs`), the methods and members of `Entity` and `Bridge`, and the callbacks the engine calls on instances. `describe_api()` reads it from a live VM; `Entity` members moved from a `strcmp` chain into one property table so the runtime and the description cannot drift; writable members are separate symbols (`Entity.position=`) so making one read-only counts as a removal. Every version is frozen as `engine/script/api/victus_script_api_<v>.json`. `victus_script_tests` checks the newest snapshot equals the live bindings and each consecutive pair follows the rules (removal or narrowing needs a major bump, an addition a minor bump, versions never go down or change without an API change); the failure lists the differing symbols and the version needed. `victus.project` records `"script_api"`; new projects get the current version and files without the key read as 1.0 (the surface frozen by this ADR). `ScriptHost::create` refuses an incompatible requirement with `VersionMismatch`; `victus check` reports it as a project error; `victus info`, `victus version` print it; `victus script-api [--out|--write|--check]` prints, freezes and checks snapshots.
- Alternatives: version only the engine (every release would be "maybe breaking"); a hand-maintained list of symbols (drifts from the bindings, which is the failure this prevents); generated Luau type definitions as the snapshot (also covers signatures, but needs every binding annotated first; can be layered on later); semver with a patch number (patches cannot change the surface, so they carry no information for scripts).
- Limits: argument lists, argument types and the shape of returned tables are not in the snapshot; they are kept by review and the behaviour tests. Deprecation warnings for symbols scheduled for removal are not implemented yet (there are none; the first major bump needs them).
- Consequences: adding a binding now also means raising the minor version and writing a snapshot in the same commit. Version 1.0 freezes the API as of this commit.

### ADR-0156: Script call budgets charge thread CPU time

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** The per-call script time budget (ADR-0008) compared wall-clock time against a deadline. On loaded CI runners (macOS Debug) a healthy `fixed_update` was killed when its thread was descheduled; blocking native calls had the same effect. Measuring the thread CPU clock on every interrupt check costs about 222 ns against 29 ns for `steady_clock` on this machine.
- **Decision:** Keep the cheap wall-clock check as the fast path. Read the thread CPU clock once after 1/8 of the budget and afterwards only while wall time is over the limit; expire the call when charged CPU time (CPU since the first read plus at most 1/8 budget of earlier wall time) exceeds the limit. Pauses (`BudgetPauseScope`) credit wall and CPU time.
- **Alternatives:** Larger default budget (hides real runaway loops longer and still flakes); pure CPU-time checks (8x more expensive per check); a watchdog thread (cross-thread interrupts, more complexity).
- **Evidence:** CI run 44858500077 failure; `victus_script_tests` budget case (blocked 300 ms on a 50 ms budget survives, busy loops still stop).
- **Consequences:** Budgets now mean CPU time; a script blocked in a slow native call is not stopped by the budget (native calls must bound themselves). On Windows the CPU clock is coarse (~15.6 ms ticks), so expiry there may land up to one tick late.

### ADR-0157: GPU device-loss recovery by rebuilding the GPU layer

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21). Player only; the editor keeps autosave-and-exit.
- **Context:** Blocker B3: a lost GPU device ended the game (exit code 3). SDL_GPU 3.4 has no device-lost or reset notification and no way to revive a device, so loss is inferred from failed operations (rhi::DeviceHealth). Game state (ECS scene, scripts, physics, audio, network) is CPU-side; GPU state is derived from assets and the scene.
- **Decision:** Treat the whole GPU layer as disposable. On loss, at the start of the next frame, destroy the scene view, frame target, cached GPU assets, GPU context and device in that order, create a new device from the same description (retrying ~10 s), attach the same window and rebuild context, cache contents (`GpuAssetCache::rebind`) and view with the previous render settings. Bounded by `device_recoveries` (default 3); then, or if rebuilding fails, exit with the loss as before.
- **Alternatives:** Recreate SDL_GPU objects in place inside rhi::Device behind stable handles (every higher layer would still hold stale pipelines and baked data, and the GLES backend would need the same); restart the process and reload the last checkpoint (loses unsaved state and multiplayer sessions); wait for upstream SDL support (no timeline).
- **Evidence:** `victus_device_loss`: three injected failure kinds recover; final frame vs clean run FLIP <= 0.010, SSIM >= 0.991 (2D), FLIP 0.0031, SSIM 0.9977 (3D, manual run).
- **Consequences:** The first frames after a recovery re-upload assets (a hitch, async loading where enabled). Objects outside the player that cache GPU handles must be rebuilt by their owner; runtime `add_model` models are dropped. A GLES/Vulkan switch would need a new window and is refused. Real driver resets are untested.

### ADR-0158: Ragdoll get-up by moving the root and blending from the lying pose

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** A passive or powered ragdoll (ADR-0091) could fall but never recover: switching back to Kinematic snapped every bone to the animated pose around the root's old position, which stayed where the character stood before it fell (the root never follows the simulation). Characters in production games stand back up from where they lie, facing the way a get-up animation expects, without a visible pop.
- **Decision:** `PhysicsWorld::begin_ragdoll_get_up(scene, root, blend_seconds = 0.4)` (Luau `entity:ragdoll_get_up([blend])`, Studio Inspector "Get Up" in play mode). It requires a passive or powered ragdoll that has simulated. The hips' rotation from the animated to the simulated pose carries the body's front (`RagdollComponent::front`, local -Z by default, serialized) and spine: front pointing up means lying on the back (`face_up`). The root moves to the ground under the hips (a raycast that ignores the character; without ground, the hips height below them) and turns to face the feet when face up (sitting up towards them) and the head when face down (pushing up). The lying world pose is snapshot and re-expressed under the moved root, the mode becomes Kinematic, and for `blend_seconds` the bone Transforms and the kinematic bodies follow a smoothstep blend from that snapshot to the animated pose (position lerp, rotation slerp, parent-local). The final frame writes the animated pose exactly and then the animation owns the bones. Setting a non-kinematic mode during the blend cancels it. `RagdollState::getting_up` and `get_up_progress` report it. The caller plays the matching get-up clip in the same frame; VICTUS does not ship get-up clips.
- **Alternatives:** Snap to the animation (visible pop, the problem being fixed); move the root continuously during simulation (a separate root/capsule follow feature; still needed for controllers, but the get-up placement is the same calculation done once); match the lying pose against a library of get-up clip first frames and pick the closest (better for curled-up poses, needs authored clips and a pose-distance metric; can be layered on `face_up`); powered recovery that balances the body up physically (active ragdoll; much harder, out of scope).
- **Evidence:** `victus_physics_tests`: 16-bone humanoid knocked back (+Z) and forward (-Z) onto a ground box: `face_up` matches the fall, root within 5 cm of the hips (XZ) and on the ground, facing within ~45 deg of the expected direction, first blended frame within 6 cm of the lying bones, progress > 0.4 halfway, final local poses equal the animated pose (< 1 mm, rotation dot > 0.9999), hips 1 m above the ground, raycasts hit the standing kinematic bodies; rejected for kinematic/no ragdoll; a knock-down during the blend cancels it. `victus_script_tests` (binding, script API 1.1) and `victus_editor_tests` (Ragdoll Lab: Orion falls, Get Up raises the head more than 0.5 m).
- **Consequences:** Script API 1.1 (adds `Entity.ragdoll_get_up`; `ragdoll_state` gains two fields). The blend is linear in parent-local space, so with a long blend feet can slide or dip slightly into the ground; 0.2-0.5 s with a get-up clip hides this. A character lying on its side picks face up/down by the sign of the front's vertical component only.

### ADR-0159: Weighted bone masks on animation layers

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Layer masks (ADR-0074) were binary: a bone was inside a layer at its full weight or outside it. An upper-body aim or shoot layer then either twists the hips with the chest or snaps at one spine joint; production rigs spread the motion over several joints (Unreal's per-bone blend depth in Layered Blend per Bone, Unity's Avatar masks combined with layer weights, Godot's per-bone blend filters).
- **Decision:** `ControllerLayer::mask_weights`, JSON `"mask_weights": { "<node>": 0..1 }` (ordered, round-trips). A node's influence is decided by its nearest listed ancestor (or itself) across `mask` (1), `exclude` (0) and `mask_weights` (the value); a name may be in only one list (parse error otherwise). Without a `mask`, weighted names act as the mask (unlisted nodes are outside), so an aim layer needs only the weights. The per-node influence (already a float) multiplies the layer weight for override blends and scales the delta for additive ones. The Studio Animation window gains a Layers section (state, fade, weight slider via `Editor::set_animation_layer_weight`, mask entries with weight bars) and `animation_debug_lines` reports layers.
- **Alternatives:** Unreal-style blend depth (weight falls off by hierarchy distance; less explicit, hard to author for branching chains); a separate mask asset shared by layers (useful for reuse, not needed for the first step); per-bone curves over time (a sync/curve feature, not a mask).
- **Evidence:** `victus_animation_tests` "weighted masks": Spine 0.25 gives 1 + 0.25 x 4 = 2 under Aim, descendants inherit, layer weight 0.5 halves it, mixed mask/exclude cases, additive scaling (+2 of +4), round-trip and three parse errors. `victus_editor_tests` Animation window: a layered controller on the 3D template robot shows `layer Upper override state Wave weight 1.00 nodes N>0`, the slider path sets 0.50.
- **Consequences:** Script API unchanged. Masks are still resolved by node name per model, so the same controller can drive differently named rigs only through retargeting.

### ADR-0160: Synced animation layers follow the base layer's phase

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Each layer advanced its own playback clock. An upper-body cycle authored to match the walk (limping, carrying, injured arm swing) drifted out of step as soon as the base blend space changed speed or its clips had different lengths. Unreal solves this with sync groups/markers, Unity with synced layers; both keep follower cycles at the leader's phase.
- **Decision:** `ControllerLayer::sync` (JSON `"sync": true`). After the layer advances, the playback of its current (and fading-out) state, when that state loops, is set to the base layer's current normalized time (and the previous value, so exit-time crossings behave like the base's). A transition into a looping state of a synced layer enters at the base phase instead of `offset`. One-shot layer states (shoot, wave) keep their own clock. Studio's Layers section marks synced layers.
- **Alternatives:** Sync markers (foot-down events in clips; exact for clips with different step timing, needs marker authoring and import; a later step); syncing to an arbitrary named layer (no request yet); speed-matching instead of phase-matching (keeps the drift).
- **Evidence:** `victus_animation_tests` "synced layer": base at 2 cycles/s, 4 s layer clip: synced layer normalized time equals the base's 0.4 after 0.2 s and the masked bone samples 0.4 x 8 = 3.2; unsynced it is 0.05 and 0.4; round-trip and a type error.
- **Consequences:** Phase matching assumes the layer clip's cycle is authored to match the base cycle; with blend spaces whose clips have different contact timing, sync markers are still needed.

### ADR-0161: Cone joint limits for FABRIK chains

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** The FABRIK chain solver (ADR-0073) kept bone lengths but allowed any angle, so a tail, spine or tentacle reaching for a target behind its root folded back through itself. FABRIK's authors describe joint constraints as a reorientation of each bone inside a cone around the previous one during the forward pass (Aristidou & Lasenby 2011, section 4).
- **Decision:** `solve_fabrik(..., max_bend)` and `IKConstraint::max_bend` (degrees, default 180 = free, serialized and shown in the inspector). In the forward pass (and when straightening toward an unreachable target), each bone direction is turned back into the cone of `max_bend` around the previous bone; the first bone uses its input (animated) direction. Iteration stops early when a limit holds the chain still (error change below 1 % of the tolerance). One symmetric cone per chain; no per-joint or twist limits.
- **Alternatives:** Per-joint elliptical cones and twist limits (needed for shoulders and hips; more authoring, a later step); CCD (handles limits more naturally but converges unevenly and favours the end joints); Jacobian/damped least squares (smooth, but iterative matrix work per frame for every chain).
- **Evidence:** `victus_animation_tests` "FABRIK joint limits": 5-joint chain, 20 deg limit, four targets including one behind the root and one out of reach: every bend <= 20 deg (+1e-3 rad), lengths kept, root fixed, never closer than the free chain; an allowed target ends within 5 mm after 30 iterations (measured 1.7 mm; the free chain reaches 0.1 mm); 180 deg equals the unlimited result; an `IKConstraint` tail with `max_bend` 25 and a target behind it keeps every world-space bend within 25 deg.
- **Consequences:** Limited chains need more iterations for the same accuracy. Two-bone and look-at solvers are unchanged (two-bone bends in the pole plane; look-at has `max_angle`).

### ADR-0162: Retained instance groups with cluster culling (GAP-08, slice 1)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** `SceneRenderer` is immediate-mode: every frame each copy of a static mesh is submitted, its world bounds transformed, LOD picked, tested against the main frustum and every shadow cascade, sorted, and its 112-byte instance data rebuilt and uploaded once per pass that draws it. A city (thousands of buildings, props, lamp posts, parked cars) pays this per copy per frame on a phone CPU. Unity's GPU Resident Drawer keeps instance data resident and draws through BatchRendererGroup (https://docs.unity3d.com/6/Documentation/Manual/urp/reduce-rendering-work-on-cpu.html); Unreal's instanced static meshes keep per-instance data on the component (https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/instanced-static-mesh-component-in-unreal-engine). Gap register: GAP-08 in `docs/audits/ENGINE_LANDSCAPE_AND_GAP_AUDIT.md`.
- **Decision:** `SceneRenderer::create_instance_group({mesh, material, models, lightmap_offsets, cluster_size=64})` sorts the copies along a 30-bit Morton curve of their bounds centres, splits them into clusters, and writes their instance data into one vertex buffer uploaded on the next render. `submit_instance_group(id)` adds one draw per cluster carrying the cluster bounds and its largest-scale copy; the existing visibility, cascade, receiver-culling, probe, LOD, rain and local-shadow paths treat it as one object. Batches gained a `buffer` field: a cluster batch binds the group buffer at its first instance (works on GLES 3.0, which has no base instance), and neighbouring visible clusters merge into one draw. Group clusters sort after per-frame draws so GPU occlusion culling keeps its invariant (per-frame batches own instances [0, n) of the frame buffer); group batches are not occlusion culled. Ids are slot + 16-bit generation; stale ids are ignored. Invalid meshes, empty or non-finite transforms and mismatched lightmap offsets are rejected.
- **Alternatives:** (1) GPU compute culling writing indirect arguments for every instance — the best cull granularity, but compute is absent on the GLES 3.0 path that entry phones fall back to, and SDL_GPU indirect draws cannot set first-instance (see the occlusion pass comment), so it needs per-batch survivor compaction; kept as slice 2 on Vulkan. (2) Caching the per-frame instance buffer when submissions do not change — fragile, still pays per-copy culling and sorting. (3) Static mesh merging — removes per-copy culling and LOD entirely and multiplies memory.
- **Evidence:** `victus_gpu_tests` "gpu instance group" (3 cases, 62 assertions, lavapipe): 400 cubes as a group match `submit_instances` within 2/255 with and without GPU occlusion culling and SSAO pre-pass, the second frame is bit-identical, fewer than 8 per-frame instances uploaded (the ground plane) versus more than 400; a narrow camera over a 4,096-copy grid sees under a quarter of its 64 clusters with zero per-frame uploads; validation, stale ids and double destroy. Full `victus_gpu_tests` 205/205. `victus_bench_render` (Release, GCC, lavapipe, 2 vCPU), 16,384 cubes, Low tier, 320x180: CPU time of begin + submit + render recording 6.83 ms per frame per-frame versus 0.43 ms retained (mean of 18 frames each); instance data uploaded 23,120 versus 0; 210 of 256 clusters visible drawing 13,440 copies versus 12,790 individually visible copies (5 % more vertex work); 11 main and 17 shadow batches versus 1 and 1; whole frame 169.3 ms versus 168.6 ms p50 because lavapipe rasterisation dominates; group creation 4.2 ms.
- **Consequences:** Copies in a visible cluster are drawn even when off-screen; one LOD per cluster; no motion vectors (static only). Changing a copy means recreating the group. Groups belong to one renderer and are lost with it (device-loss recovery recreates the renderer, so owners must recreate their groups). No real-GPU or phone measurement yet.

### ADR-0163: Foot placement and foot locking

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Animated characters played their clips as authored for flat ground: on slopes, stairs and kerbs one foot floated and the other sank into the floor, and every speed/blend mismatch made planted feet slide (the showcase blueprint rejects foot sliding). Scripts could cast rays and set IK targets by hand, but every project had to rewrite the same logic and it ran after the pose, one frame late.
- **Decision:** `FootPlacement` on the character root and `FootContact` on each foot (each foot also carries a TwoBone `IKConstraint` without a target entity). Every animation update, between sampling and IK, each foot casts a ray from `probe_up` above to `probe_down` below its animated position through an `AnimationSettings::ground` probe (`runtime::physics_ground_probe` adapts the physics world and ignores the character's own bodies). The foot keeps the height the animation gives it above the root, measured from the ground under it; the pelvis drops by the largest downward correction (capped by `max_pelvis_drop`); corrections are smoothed exponentially (`smoothing`). A foot slower than `lock_speed` within `lock_height` of its rest height is pinned in XZ where it planted until it lifts or drifts `release_distance` away; it relocks only after its lock weight has faded to zero. After IK the foot tilts to the ground normal, limited by `max_slope`. Without a probe the IK targets equal the animated feet, so edit-mode previews are unchanged. `make_humanoid_foot_placement` sets up a biped in one call (Studio card and command palette); `foot_placement_lines` feeds the View > IK and Foot Placement overlay.
- **Alternatives:** A script library (one frame late, duplicated per project); full-body IK with a pelvis solve (better for big height differences, much larger); heel and toe rays per foot (better on edges of steps; a later step); sync markers to decide plant phases from the clip (needs marker authoring and import; still planned).
- **Evidence:** `victus_animation_tests` foot placement cases: step and hole under each foot with the pelvis dropping by the lower one and capped; procedural rigs not drifting over 300 frames and smoothing converging monotonically; tilt to a 20 degree slope and capped at `max_slope`; a slow planted foot staying within a millimetre while the root moves, then releasing past `release_distance`; humanoid setup on a 16-bone rig (2 feet, pelvis Hips) and clean removal; misconfigured feet counted as invalid and components round-tripping through the scene format; the physics probe planting feet on a box while ignoring the character's capsule. `victus_editor_tests` "studio feet": Add Foot Placement on the template Robot (2 feet), undo/redo, locking toggle, overlay lines in edit and play.
- **Consequences:** Script API unchanged (components are reflected, so scripts can edit fields). Ground is measured along world up only; one ray per foot; feet need a two-bone chain; locking uses the animated world speed of the foot, so root motion that teleports the root is seen as fast movement. Runtime now depends on Physics (it already linked it through the player).

### ADR-0164: Foliage draws from cached instance groups with per-cluster distance limits (GAP-08, slice 2)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Scattered foliage (ADR-0150) rebuilt every copy's world matrix, tested its distance against the draw range and shadow fraction, and handed the list to the per-frame instance path every frame; KNOWN_LIMITATIONS recorded it as "culled and LOD-selected per instance on the CPU every frame". ADR-0162 added retained groups but nobody used them, and their owner needs to know when to rebuild.
- **Decision:** `SceneRenderer::cached_instance_group(key, revision, mesh, material, build, cluster_size)` keeps groups by caller key and rebuilds with `build` only when the revision, mesh, material or cluster size changes; groups not requested for 120 `begin()` calls are released, so device-loss recovery (a new renderer) simply rebuilds them. `submit_instance_group(id, {max_distance, shadow_distance})` skips clusters whose nearest point is beyond `max_distance` and draws those beyond `shadow_distance` without shadows, and returns the copies submitted. `extract_scene` submits each foliage entity and mesh as one cached group (clusters of 32), keyed by entity and mesh, with a revision hashing the scatter settings, every chunk's build revision, the terrain transform and the mesh bounds (a hot-reloaded mesh at the same address rebuilds).
- **Alternatives:** One group per terrain chunk (cheaper repaint rebuilds but more, shorter draw runs); keeping per-instance distance tests on the CPU with a persistent buffer of survivors (exact edge, but per-copy work again); GPU compute selection (slice 3, Vulkan only).
- **Evidence:** `victus_gpu_tests` "gpu: foliage draws its mesh renderer..." (lavapipe, Release, 2 vCPU), dense scatter of 25,872 copies, steady state over 10 frames with one renderer: `extract_scene` 1.67 ms → 0.07 ms and `render()` recording 2.69 ms → 0.44 ms per frame (three runs each: 1.65/1.72/1.67 and 2.68/2.74/2.66 before; 0.07/0.07/0.08 and 0.45/0.41/0.47 after); per-frame instance uploads 5,394 → 4 (terrain). Main batches 5 → 19 for the same view (frustum-culled clusters split merged runs). With a short range on the 1,609-copy layer (camera 12 m above, 15 m range halved) 480 copies draw instead of 263, because a cluster within range draws all of its copies.
- **Consequences:** The draw-distance and shadow edges are cluster-granular: up to one cluster (32 copies, a few metres) beyond the per-instance edge, which shows most when the range is short compared with the cluster size. More draw calls (one per visible run). Painting a foliage chunk rebuilds the entity's whole group (one pass over its copies).

### ADR-0165: Ordered, rate-limited systems and simulation LOD tiers (GAP-09)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** `gameplay::Simulation` ran a flat list of systems in four phases in registration order; every system ran every pass and every agent updated every frame. A city with thousands of pedestrians and vehicles cannot afford full-rate behaviour trees and animation for agents a kilometre away, but they must keep living ("whether you are looking or not"). Unreal Mass groups stateless processors into tick phases with declared ordering and drives detail from LOD/significance (https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/overview-of-mass-entity-in-unreal-engine?application_version=5.0); Unity Entities orders systems with `UpdateBefore`/`UpdateAfter` attributes (https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.entities@1.0/manual/systems-update-order.html). Gap register: GAP-09 in `docs/audits/ENGINE_LANDSCAPE_AND_GAP_AUDIT.md`.
- **Decision:** (1) `add_system(name, phase, fn, SystemOptions{after, before, interval, offset})`; the two-argument form is unchanged. Within a phase each system is placed in registration order immediately after the systems it must follow (a depth-first stable topological order, so a constraint moves as few systems as possible). Cycles keep registration order for their members and dependents and are reported by `schedule_diagnostics()`, as are names not registered in the same phase (optional modules); the player logs them at start-up. `interval` runs a system every Nth pass of its phase with the summed delta of skipped passes; `offset` staggers systems sharing an interval. (2) `SimulationLod` (per entity: `bias`, `always_full`) and `SimulationLodSource` (`radius_scale`, `enabled`) components, reflected and serialized; scene resource `SimulationLodSettings` (tier distances 30/80/200 m, intervals 1/2/4/8 frames, 10 % hysteresis, primary-camera fallback). The built-in `simlod.assign` PreUpdate system puts each entity in a tier by distance to the nearest source, decides whether it is due this frame (staggered by a hash of the entity index), and accumulates its delta so a due entity simulates exactly the time since its last update. `simulation_lod_tick(component, dt)` returns `{due, dt}`; entities without the component are always due with the frame delta. Consumers in this change: AI behaviour trees (`AiStats::lod_skipped`), animators (`AnimationStats::lod_skipped`, skipped animators keep their pose), and the built-in Rotator and Oscillator. Player profile counters `sim.lod_entities`, `sim.lod_due`, `sim.lod_tier0..3`.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Per-system LOD buckets (each processor keeps its own entity lists per tier): finer control but every system duplicates the bucketing; one shared assignment per frame is cheaper and keeps consumers consistent. (2) A dependency graph executing independent systems in parallel on the job system: needs declared read/write sets for every existing system, which the 35 current registrations do not have; ordering constraints are the prerequisite and parallel phases can follow. (3) Time-sliced budgets (update as many far agents as fit in N ms): adaptive but nondeterministic; fixed intervals keep replays and lockstep tests deterministic.
- **Evidence:** `victus_gameplay_tests` (9 cases, 370 assertions, Release, GCC 15.2, 2 vCPU): ordering with after/before and an unrelated phase, cycle reporting, interval systems with offsets accounting for every frame, tier boundaries and hysteresis, 128 agents with per-frame due counts between 64 and 88 (64 near + far staggered over 8 frames) and per-entity simulated time equal to wall time after a flush, camera fallback, source radius scale, bias, always_full, no accumulation while paused, a far Rotator turning by the same total angle as a near one, round-trip through the scene format; `simlod.assign` for 20,000 agents in 0.37 ms per frame. `victus_ai_tests` new case: a far behaviour tree skips most frames yet finishes a 1 s wait no more than one interval (8 frames) after the near one.
- **Consequences:** Far agents react up to one interval late (8 frames, 133 ms at 60 Hz by default). Tiers use last frame's world transforms. IK and foot placement still run every frame on skipped animators' held poses. Physics (FixedUpdate) and scripts do not follow LOD yet; scripts can read the components' fields through reflection. Distance is to the nearest source only; no screen-size or visibility term yet (A.E.G.I.S. significance can feed `bias` later).

### ADR-0166: Wheeled vehicles on Jolt's vehicle constraint (GAP-24)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** VICTUS had no vehicle physics; the template's Special Ops Mule moves kinematically. The city showcase needs a drivable player car with suspension, steering, a gearbox and brakes. Unreal ships Chaos Vehicles (https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/how-to-set-up-vehicles-in-unreal-engine), Unity has `WheelCollider` (https://docs.unity3d.com/Manual/class-WheelCollider.html) and Godot `VehicleBody3D`/`VehicleWheel3D` (https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/classes/class_vehiclebody3d.html). Jolt, already the 3D backend (ADR-0009), has `VehicleConstraint` with a `WheeledVehicleController` (engine, automatic transmission, differentials, anti-roll bars, Pacejka-style friction curves) (https://jrouwe.github.io/JoltPhysics/class_vehicle_constraint.html). Gap register: GAP-24.
- **Decision:** `VehicleComponent` ("Vehicle": engine torque, rpm range, shift points, differential ratio, limited-slip ratio, pitch/roll limit, anti-roll stiffness, `player_input`) on an entity with a dynamic `RigidBody` and `Collider` (the chassis), and `VehicleWheelComponent` ("VehicleWheel": radius, width, suspension length and travel, spring frequency and damping, maximum steering angle, driven, brake and handbrake torque, grip) on its direct children, mirroring Godot's body/wheel split. The chassis frame is +Z forward, +Y up (the glTF convention); each wheel's authored position is its centre at full droop. The Jolt backend builds one `VehicleConstraint` per chassis: wheels sharing a Z within 10 cm form an axle with an anti-roll bar, driven wheels on an axle share a limited-slip differential and the engine torque is split evenly across differentials; wheels are tested with a cylinder cast on the chassis' object layer. The constraint is rebuilt when either component changes or the chassis body is rebuilt (authored rest positions are kept, because the simulation writes the wheel Transforms). After each step the wheels' world poses (suspension, steering, spin about local X) are written back. API: `PhysicsWorld::set_vehicle_input(e, {forward, steer, brake, handbrake})` (persistent, clamped), `vehicle_input(e)`, `vehicle_state(e)` (`valid`, forward `speed`, `engine_rpm`, `gear`, `wheels_in_contact`, per-wheel contact/suspension/steer/rpm, `error`). `player_input` vehicles are driven by the PreUpdate system `physics.vehicle_input` through `vehicle_input_from` (W/S/A/D, arrows, Space, gamepad triggers, left stick and South; pressing against the direction of travel brakes before reversing), honouring `InputRouting` for networked sessions.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Raycast-suspension vehicles written in VICTUS (springs and friction forces applied to a rigid body): small and backend-neutral, but it would duplicate Jolt's tested tyre model, transmission and differentials. (2) Wheels as separate rigid bodies on hinge joints: physically literal but unstable at speed and expensive; every engine above uses a constraint or cast model. (3) A single "car" component with a fixed four-wheel layout: simpler to author but excludes motorbikes, trucks and six-wheelers; child wheels cost one extra entity each and match imported car models, whose wheels are usually separate nodes.
- **Evidence:** `victus_physics_tests -tc="vehicle*"`: the AWD car reaches 20.59 m/s after 5 s of full throttle (gear 2, 4,102 rpm); a 500 N·m RWD car stays in gear 1 at 6,000 rpm / 16.7 m/s because Jolt holds the gear while a driven wheel slips; the car settles with 4 wheels in contact (wheel centre 0.35 m, chassis 0.505 m).
- **Consequences:** Only wheeled vehicles (no tracked vehicles or motorcycles with lean control yet; Jolt has both controllers). 2D physics has no vehicles. The wheel mesh must turn about its local X axis. Wheels are cast against the chassis' collision layer mask. Vehicles do not follow simulation LOD; distant traffic is meant to use kinematic lane following (GAP-22). No Studio vehicle wizard yet.

### ADR-0167: Lane-graph traffic with signals and IDM agents (GAP-22)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** A city needs ambient vehicles that follow roads, queue, obey lights and turn at junctions, in the thousands. Unreal's City Sample does this with ZoneGraph lanes (https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/API/Plugins/ZoneGraph, `UZoneGraphSubsystem` lane queries) and Mass traffic; Unity and Godot ship nothing comparable in-engine. Car following is a solved modelling problem: the Intelligent Driver Model (Treiber, Hennecke and Helbing, Phys. Rev. E 62, 1805, 2000, https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevE.62.1805; summary https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intelligent_driver_model) is collision-free with a few parameters. Physics vehicles (ADR-0166) are too expensive for background traffic. Gap register: GAP-22.
- **Decision:** New layer-5 module `engine/traffic`. A `RoadNetwork` (nodes and two-way or one-way roads with lane counts, lane width and speed limit; JSON `.vroads`, `"format": "victus.roads"`, version 1; `grid_network` generator) is compiled by `build_lane_graph` into a `LaneGraph`: straight driving lanes offset to the right (or left with `left_hand`) and trimmed by a per-node clearance radius, cubic-Bezier connector lanes from every arriving lane to the leaving lanes of every other road (lane i to lane min(i, n-1); U-turns only at dead ends), connector speed capped by curvature (3 m/s^2 lateral), and a split-phase `Signal` at every node with 3+ roads (each approach green, amber, all-red in turn; deterministic offsets). `TrafficAgent` entities are kinematic: per frame the system snapshots lane occupancy, finds the nearest obstacle (vehicle ahead on the lane or the lanes after it, a red light or an amber one the car can stop for, or a car nearer a merge), applies IDM acceleration a[1-(v/v0)^4-(s*/s)^2] with v0 limited by the lane and the braking distance to a slower next lane, integrates in sub-steps of at most 0.1 s, picks the next lane from a per-agent hash, and writes `Transform`. Agents honour simulation LOD (ADR-0165). The authored `TrafficNetwork` component builds a grid or loads a `.vroads` file when play starts and spawns agents, copying its first child as the vehicle template. Registered as `traffic.update` (Update) in the player and Studio play mode.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Agents on the Recast crowd (navigation module): crowd steering does not keep lanes, signals or headway and was measured at about 1 ms per 128 agents (STATUS slice 16). (2) Physics vehicles driven by an AI controller: realistic but costs a Jolt constraint per car; kept for the player and nearby hero cars. (3) Cellular-automaton traffic (Nagel-Schreckenberg): cheaper, but discrete cells look jerky at street level.
- **Evidence:** `victus_traffic_tests` (see STATUS.md 2026-11-21): a lone car reaches the 13.9 m/s limit and U-turns at a dead end; 120 cars on a 4x4 grid over 180 s never come closer than 1.99 m on a lane, up to 32 wait at lights at once, all keep moving and two runs are bit-identical; 2,000 agents on a 10x10 two-lane grid update in 0.42 ms per frame (Release, 2 vCPU).
- **Consequences:** No lane changes on multi-lane roads (a car stays in its lane index through junctions), no protected turn phases (split phase only, so throughput at busy crossings is low), no conflict checks between crossing paths beyond merges (the all-red interval clears the box), no pedestrians or crossings, no route planning to a destination, no physics interaction with the player car (cars do not react to bodies), graph lives in the TrafficNetwork entity's local space, and no Studio road editor or graph overlay yet.

### ADR-0168: Tiled navmesh with per-tile incremental rebuilds (GAP-23)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** The navmesh was one Recast tile. Levels above 4,096 x 4,096 voxels got coarser voxels (losing doorways), the whole mesh was rebuilt whenever any static collider moved, and the build ran on one thread. Unreal builds navmesh in tiles and regenerates only dirty tiles at run time (https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/optimizing-navigation-mesh-generation-speed-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US, world-partitioned navmesh: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/world-partitioned-navigation-mesh); Unity's NavMesh and Godot also tile. Detour supports multi-tile meshes with `init(dtNavMeshParams)`, `addTile`, `removeTile` and `getTileRefAt` (https://recastnav.com/classdtNavMesh.html), shown in RecastDemo's `Sample_TileMesh.cpp` (https://github.com/recastnavigation/recastnavigation/blob/main/RecastDemo/Source/Sample_TileMesh.cpp). Gap register: GAP-23.
- **Decision:** `NavMeshSurfaceComponent::tile_size` (metres; 0 = automatic). Automatic mode keeps the existing single-tile path when the level has at most 1,048,576 voxel columns and otherwise tiles at 256 voxels per side; a positive `tile_size` always tiles (32 to 1,024 voxels). The tile grid origin is snapped to multiples of the tile size, and tiles double in size while the grid exceeds 4,096 tiles (32-bit poly refs: tile plus polygon bits must fit in 22). Tiled builds keep the authored voxel size up to 65,536 voxels per side. The gather records each collider and height field as a source with bounds and an FNV-1a hash of its vertices; each tile hashes the surface settings and the sources overlapping it (with the `agent radius + 3` voxel border). When the grid is unchanged, only tiles whose hash changed are cooked and swapped into the live mesh with `removeTile`/`addTile`, and crowd agents are re-registered on the patched mesh; otherwise a fresh multi-tile mesh is created. Triangles are bucketed per tile by their bounds, and tiles are cooked by a pool of up to `NavigationSettings::build_threads` (0 = hardware threads, at most 8) workers pulling from an atomic counter; the async build path (ADR-0119) runs the same job off the main thread. Queries get 16,384 search nodes on tiled meshes. `NavStats::tiles` and `tiles_rebuilt` report the live tile count and the tiles cooked by the last build.
- **Alternatives:** (1) DetourTileCache with compressed layers and temporary obstacles: carving cylinders and boxes is cheaper than re-cooking a tile, but it needs a second build pipeline and only supports simple obstacle shapes; tile hashes already confine general geometry changes. (2) Keep single tile and only coarsen: loses narrow passages on large maps. (3) Per-streaming-cell navmeshes (Unreal world partition): needs cross-cell tile ownership; left as the next step on this grid.
- **Evidence:** `victus_navigation_tests` (13 cases, 613 assertions, Release, GCC 15.2, 2 vCPU): a 20 m arena forced to 6 m tiles routes through the same gap as the single-tile mesh within 3 m of length; a 200 m x 200 m level with nine walls builds 36 tiles, a 254.6 m diagonal path across tile borders matches the straight line within 3 %, walls bend paths; adding a 2 m crate re-cooks 1 tile and paths go around it; an agent keeps walking across tile borders through an incremental rebuild and arrives. Timings over three runs: full tiled build 256-268 ms, one-crate rebuild 19.1-19.8 ms; the same level as a single tile (threshold disabled): 647 ms build, 688 ms rebuild.
- **Consequences:** The geometry gather and change signature still visit every collider each check; tiles are not streamed with world cells; kinematic obstacles re-cook their tiles (no TileCache carving); agents are re-registered after every incremental rebuild, which resets their corridors (they replan the same frame). Navmesh polygons can differ slightly at tile borders from a single-tile build.

### ADR-0169: UI widgets, text input and rich text (GAP-29, slice 1)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** In-game UI had panels, images, text, buttons, layouts and scroll views, but no value widgets, no text entry and single-style text. Unity UI Toolkit ships `Slider`, `Toggle`, `TextField` and `ProgressBar` controls (https://docs.unity.com/en-us/engine/7000.0/manual/uitoolkits/uielements/uie-element-ref, https://docs.unity.com/en-us/engine/6000.7/script-reference/unityengine/uielements/slider, https://docs.unity.com/en-us/engine/6000.6/script-reference/unityengine/uielements/textfield) and TextMesh Pro rich text tags (https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.textmeshpro@4.0/manual/RichTextSupportedTags.html); Unreal UMG has Slider, CheckBox, EditableText and ProgressBar widgets (https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/python-api/class/EditableText?application_version=5.2). Gap register: GAP-29.
- **Decision:** Four reflected components in `engine/gameui`: `UiSlider` (min/max/step snapping, horizontal or vertical, drag captures the pointer until release, Left/Right or A/D step the focused slider, a slider press never starts a scroll drag), `UiToggle` (click or Enter flips it), `UiTextInput` (single line; click or Enter starts editing, platform UTF-8 text is inserted at the caret, Backspace/Delete/Left/Right/Home/End move by code point, Enter submits, Escape, clicking elsewhere or hiding the field ends editing, `max_length` in code points, password masking, caret blink every 30 updates, the view scrolls to keep the caret visible) and `UiProgressBar`. Value changes and submits are `UiEvent`s with `kind` Changed/Submitted plus `value` and `text`, delivered to Luau `on_value_changed(widget, action, value)` and `on_submit(widget, action, text)` on the widget or any ancestor, like `on_click`. Entity `ui_value()`/`set_ui_value(v)` read and write any widget. `gameplay::InputSource::text_input()` carries typed text; the player turns SDL text input on only while a field is edited and does not quit on Escape then. `UiText::rich` parses `<b>`, `<color=#rrggbb[aa]|name>` and their closing tags into runs (unknown tags stay literal); the renderer lays runs out word by word with the Medium and Bold fonts and wraps them.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Retained widget tree separate from the ECS (UMG/UI Toolkit style): duplicates layout and serialization already on components. (2) Immediate-mode Dear ImGui widgets in games: not themeable, no scene serialization, no gamepad focus model. (3) Full TextMesh Pro tag set (size, sprites, links): left for later; bold and colour cover HUD emphasis.
- **Evidence:** `victus_gameui_tests` 15 cases / 301 assertions (slider drag, snap, keyboard step and change events; toggles, progress bars, event routing; text field caret edits with multi-byte text, submit, cancel, limits; rich-text run parsing); `victus_gpu_tests` "gpu ui: widgets, text field caret and rich text runs draw" (slider fill and handle, toggle track, red and white rich-text runs, caret pixels, image inspected); `victus_script_tests` "script: UI widgets report on_value_changed and on_submit" (16 assertions, real clicks and typed text). Benchmark in STATUS.md.
- **Consequences:** No text selection, clipboard, multi-line fields, IME candidate window placement or key repeat for Backspace; Studio play mode does not enable platform text input; the UI is still authored in the Inspector, not a visual canvas (next GAP-29 slice).

### ADR-0170: Surface shader vertex functions (GAP-17, slice 1)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** `.vshader` surface shaders (ADR-0118) only ran per pixel, so wind on foliage, waving flags, water swell, vertex animation and pulsing props were impossible without engine changes. Godot spatial shaders take an optional `vertex()` function that edits `VERTEX`, `NORMAL`, `UV` and `COLOR` before projection (https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/shaders/shader_reference/spatial_shader.html); Unreal materials have World Position Offset, which also moves the mesh in its shadow depth passes and does not grow culling bounds unless "Max World Position Offset Displacement" is set (https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/material-inputs?application_version=4.27, https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/unreal-engine-material-properties?application_version=5.7); Unity Shader Graph has a vertex context with Position/Normal/Tangent blocks. Gap register: GAP-17.
- **Decision:** A `.vshader` may define `void vertex(VertexInput i, inout Vertex v)` next to, or instead of, `surface()`. `VertexInput` has the object-space `position` and `normal` (after skinning), `uv`, `color`, `object_origin`, `time`, `shadow` (true while drawing shadow maps) and the material's four `params`; `Vertex` holds `world_position`, `normal`, `uv` and `color`, prefilled with the built-in transform, and is used as written. The renderer embeds a GLSL template (`engine/render/shaders/templates/surface_vertex.glsl`) and compiles five variants per shader at first use (static, skinned, lightmapped, shadow, skinned shadow) with glslang/SPIRV-Cross; the fragment build strips the vertex function (keeping line numbers) and the vertex build strips `surface()`, so errors point at the user's line. Vertex uniforms use a second slot `VertexMaterial { time; params[4] }`. A material with a vertex stage gets its own forward pipeline and its own shadow pipelines (sun cascades, local lights, rain occlusion) keyed by shader, skinning and alpha cut-out; shadow batches only merge draws with the same stage. Vertex-only shaders use the built-in surface. `compile_surface_shader` (used by Studio, `victus check` and `victus export`) also compiles the static vertex variant and refuses extra resources (more than two uniform blocks, extra samplers).
- **Alternatives:** (1) A free-form vertex shader file per material: full control but users would have to re-implement skinning, lightmap uvs, instancing and the shadow variants; the function hook keeps those in the engine. (2) Displacement only along the normal (one float output like Unity's terrain displacement): too narrow for wind or vertex animation. (3) Running the vertex function in the depth prepass and motion-vector pass too: correct for SSAO/TAA but doubles the variant count; deferred (see consequences).
- **Evidence:** `victus_gpu_tests` "gpu: surface shader vertex functions move geometry in the main and shadow passes" (lavapipe): function detection ignores comments, prototypes and longer names; stripping keeps line counts; a vertex-only shader compiles; an error is reported at `wave.vshader:3`; an extra sampler is refused; a 20 m plane moved 10 m by `params[0].x` covers only one half of the view; a cube whose shadow-pass copy is moved 2 m (`if (i.shadow)`) casts its shadow beside itself (floor red at that spot 237 with the shadow under the cube, 175 with it moved; images inspected).
- **Consequences:** Draws with a vertex stage skip the depth prepass and motion vectors, so they get no SSAO and TAA reprojects them as static; culling bounds are not grown by the offset (keep offsets small or enlarge the mesh bounds); vertex functions cannot sample textures yet; custom textures and named uniforms are the next GAP-17 slice.

### ADR-0171: Named parameters and textures in surface shaders (GAP-17, slice 2)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Surface shaders (ADR-0118, ADR-0170) could only read four anonymous `params[k]` vectors and the material's own four textures, so vertex animation textures, masks, flow maps, interior mapping and detail layers were impossible, and the Material Editor showed meaningless rows. Godot declares material inputs as file-scope `uniform` lines with hints (`source_color`, `hint_range(min, max[, step])`, `hint_default_white`, `hint_default_black`, `hint_normal`) and defaults written after the hint, which the inspector turns into editable parameters (https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/shaders/shader_reference/shading_language.html); Unity Shader Graph and Unreal material parameters work the same way through named properties. Gap register: GAP-17.
- **Decision:** A `.vshader` may declare `uniform float|vec2|vec3|vec4 name [: hint_range(min,max[,step]) | source_color] [= value];` (up to four, mapped in order onto the material's four parameter slots; `source_color` only on vec3/vec4) and `uniform sampler2D name [: hint_default_white|hint_default_black|hint_normal];` (up to four, one on GLES, whose RHI has 16 texture units per stage). The engine rewrites each declaration in place (line numbers kept): parameters become `#define`s onto the fragment `material.custom[k]` or vertex `vx_material.params[k]` components, textures become layout bindings (fragment set 2 bindings 12+k after the eight PBR samplers, vertex set 0 bindings k), and the light, cluster and probe storage buffers and the skinning palette shift by `VX_CUSTOM_TEXTURES`, so `pbr.frag` and the vertex template stay one source. Both `surface()` and `vertex()` can read them. Materials store `shader_textures` (UUIDs, JSON array omitted when empty) and the number of explicitly set parameter slots; unset slots take the declared default when the material is resolved, and unassigned textures bind white, black or a flat normal. Material keys and batching include the custom textures; shadow passes bind the vertex ones. The Material Editor shows declared parameters by name (colour pickers, range sliders with step snapping, drag fields) and texture slots with drag-and-drop; the raw slots stay under a fold-out. Declaration errors (duplicates, unknown hints, reserved `gl_`/`u_`/`vx_`/`material`/`VICTUS` names, bad defaults, too many) are reported at the user's line.
- **Alternatives:** (1) A side-car JSON parameter schema per shader: two files to keep in sync. (2) A per-material uniform block sized by the shader: more than four parameters, but every material variant would need its own buffer layout and the batching key would grow; four vec4 slots already match the existing material block. (3) Texture arrays for custom textures: one binding, but all layers must share size and format.
- **Evidence:** `victus_gpu_tests` "gpu: surface shader named parameters and textures reach both stages" (lavapipe): parsing of ranges, steps, colours, defaults, splatted `vec2(2)`, line numbers, comment skipping, seven rejected forms; a duplicate reported at `dup.vshader:3`; two textures refused on GLES at `two.vshader:2`, one accepted; four textures used only in `vertex()` compile. A 20 m unlit plane with `tint = vec3(0,0,1)` and a black-default mask renders blue on both halves; with slot 0 set to green and a red texture assigned, the vertex function (`textureLod(mask, vec2(0.5), 0.0).r * 10.0`) moves the plane 10 m so one half shows the clear colour and the other red + green (pixels 0x9f9f9f vs 0xe6ff00 RGB); `shader_textures` round-trips through `.vmat`.
- **Consequences:** Still four parameter slots and no int/bool/array/matrix uniforms; custom textures use the material's sampler (filter/tiling) and are not marked sRGB by a hint (the texture's import setting decides); the GLES limit of one custom texture remains until the GLES RHI gets more units; remaining GAP-17 work is vertex functions in the depth prepass and motion vectors and culling bounds growth.

### ADR-0172: glTF lights and cameras in imported models (GAP-30, slice 1)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** The glTF importer dropped `KHR_lights_punctual` lights and `cameras`, so a lit room or a camera rig authored in Blender arrived dark and without its shots; Unity, Unreal (Interchange) and Godot all import them. The extension defines point/spot intensity in candela and directional in lux, an infinite range when `range` is absent, spot cone defaults 0 and pi/4, emission along local -Z, and light properties unaffected by node scale (https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/blob/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_lights_punctual/README.md). Blender's exporter writes sun strength (W/m²) times 683 and point/spot watts / 4pi times 683 in its default "Standard" mode (https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO/blob/main/addons/io_scene_gltf2/blender/exp/light.py); Godot's importer uses the glTF intensity as its light energy unchanged (https://github.com/godotengine/godot/blob/master/modules/gltf/extensions/gltf_light.cpp). VICTUS light intensities are unitless, with the default sun at 3. Gap register: GAP-30.
- **Decision:** `CookedModel` gains `lights` (`ModelLight`: type, linear colour, intensity, range in metres, cone half-angles) and `cameras` (`ModelCamera`: perspective `yfov`/aspect or orthographic `ymag`, near/far), referenced by `ModelNode::light`/`camera`; cooked format v10, model importer v8. Intensities are multiplied by the new `ModelImportSettings::light_intensity_scale` (default 1/683), which turns a Blender-exported file back into the watt-based numbers the artist typed (sun 3 W/m² -> 3), matching VICTUS's scale; files written unitless use 1. Range and clip planes follow the import scale. `instantiate_model` adds `LightComponent`s (unlimited range becomes `clamp(sqrt(100 x intensity), 1, 1000)` m, where a unit-distance light falls to 1%) and `CameraComponent`s that are never primary, so dropping a model never takes over the view or turns a 3D scene into 2D (VICTUS treats a primary orthographic camera as 2D). `import_lights`/`import_cameras` turn either off. `export_gltf` writes both back (intensity x 683) and re-imports to the same values.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Use intensity as-is like Godot: physically labelled files would arrive 683 times too bright (a 3 W/m² sun at 2049). (2) Physical light units throughout the renderer (lux/candela with camera exposure): the right long-term model, but a renderer-wide change to every light, the sky and exposure; the scale setting keeps that door open. (3) Make the first imported camera primary: surprising when a prop file happens to contain a camera.
- **Evidence:** `victus_asset_tests` "assets: glTF lights and cameras import, round-trip and instantiate": sun 2049 lux -> 3.0, spot 683 cd range 12 at import scale 2 -> 1.0 and 24 m with cones 0.2/0.5 rad, unnamed point -> `light2`, perspective camera near/far scaled to 0.1/500, orthographic `ymag` 4 -> 8 with aspect 2; cooked round trip and an out-of-range light index rejected; settings off import none and round-trip through `.meta` JSON; instantiation yields 3 lights (spot 28.65/11.46 degrees, derived range sqrt(1000)) and 2 non-primary cameras (34.38 degrees, ortho height 16); export then re-import gives the same values.
- **Consequences:** Only the first directional light in a scene drives the sun, as before; area lights, light textures and FBX are not covered (FBX import is the next GAP-30 slice); camera aspect ratio is stored but the view still uses the viewport's; orthographic model cameras only render when made primary in a 2D scene.

### ADR-0173: FBX and OBJ import through ufbx (GAP-30, slice 2)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Models from Maya, 3ds Max, Cinema 4D, the Unity and Unreal marketplaces and most asset stores arrive as FBX, and simple props as OBJ; VICTUS only read glTF, so users had to round-trip through Blender. Unity and Unreal import FBX directly (Unreal's Interchange and FBX pipelines, Unity's FBX importer); Godot 4.3+ imports FBX natively with ufbx, applying unit conversion to the mesh rather than node scale and folding geometric pivots (https://godotengine.org/article/introducing-the-improved-ufbx-importer-in-godot-4-3/, https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/assets_pipeline/importing_3d_scenes/available_formats.html). The Autodesk FBX SDK is closed source with a restrictive licence and no static Linux/ARM builds suited to a shipped cooker. ufbx (https://github.com/ufbx/ufbx, v0.23.1, single C file, MIT or public domain) reads binary and ASCII FBX 3000-7700 and OBJ/MTL, is fuzzed upstream, and converts axes, units, pivots, geometric transforms and inherit modes for the caller. Gap register: GAP-30.
- **Decision:** Vendor ufbx v0.23.1 unmodified in `third_party/ufbx` (built as the static C library `victus_ufbx` without VICTUS warning flags, like cgltf) and add `import_fbx` / `import_fbx_memory` in `engine/assets/src/fbx_import.cpp`, producing the same `CookedModel` as glTF. Loading uses target axes +X right, +Y up, +Z back (forward -Z) for the scene, cameras and lights, `target_unit_meters = 1`, space conversion, geometric transforms and pivots folded into geometry and plain TRS nodes (`MODIFY_GEOMETRY`, `ADJUST_TO_PIVOT`, inherit-mode compensation), generated missing normals. External files (OBJ `.mtl`) are opened through a callback that only reads inside the asset directory and records them as dependencies; textures load from embedded content, then the relative path, then the bare file name beside the model or in `textures/`, inside the sandbox only. Meshes are split per material part, triangulated with `ufbx_triangulate_face` and de-duplicated with `ufbx_generate_indices` over the vertex plus its control point, so skin weights and blend shapes stay attached; uv v is flipped to the glTF convention; then the glTF path's tangents, LODs and vertex-cache optimisation run. Materials use ufbx's PBR mapping (base colour x factor, roughness or inverted glossiness, metalness, emission x factor, normal map, opacity as alpha test, double-sided, unlit). Skins take cluster bones and `geometry_to_bone` as inverse bind; vertices without any weight (valid in Maya/Max, where they stay rigid) bind to an extra joint that is the mesh node itself. Blend shape channels become morph targets with their `DeformPercent` animation as a weights channel (0-100 -> 0-1). Animation stacks are baked with `ufbx_bake_anim` (30 Hz resample, key reduction, start trimmed) into translation/rotation/scale channels, skipping constant channels equal to the rest pose. Lights (point, spot, directional; area/volume as point with a warning; FBX full cone angles halved) and cameras (field of view, near/far, orthographic size) map onto ADR-0172's `ModelLight`/`ModelCamera`; FBX light intensity is used as-is (ufbx already divides the stored percentage by 100), so `light_intensity_scale` does not apply. `.fbx`/`.obj` are model assets in the database (importer key stays `gltf` so `.meta` settings are shared), Studio drops of `.obj` copy their `mtllib` and the maps it names into one folder, and models that contain only lights or cameras are now valid for glTF and FBX.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Autodesk FBX SDK: most compatible, but proprietary, large, dynamic and not redistributable in an open engine's cooker. (2) Assimp: broad format list, but heavy (hundreds of thousands of lines), slower, BSD with many bundled dependencies and weaker FBX animation/pivot handling. (3) FBX2glTF conversion at import (Godot 3.x/4.0): an external executable per platform and a lossy second hop.
- **Evidence:** `victus_asset_tests` `fbx_import_tests.cpp` 7 cases / 168 assertions on unmodified ufbx corpus files in `tests/data/fbx` (Release, GCC 15.2): Maya ASCII 7.5 cube (24 vertices, 36 indices, `lambert1` 0.4 grey, cube proportions kept, import scale 2 doubles the size); Blender 2.93 binary cube with 3 embedded textures cooked and upright (2 m tall, centred in Y-up); Blender half-skinned plane where bone-skinned vertices reproduce Blender's own posed OBJ export within 1 mm and unweighted ones stay on the mesh node; Maya blend-shape cube with 2 morph targets and an animated 2-wide weights channel in 0..1; Maya camera/light rig with the light along -Z and a 16:9 camera; OBJ + MTL + PNG split into two materials with the texture's red pixel, v flipped, `.mtl`/`.png` recorded as dependencies, a `../` material library refused, memory import with no file reads, garbage bytes rejected; the asset database cooks an `.fbx` with morphs and animation. Each case runs in 0.3-2.5 ms. `victus_editor_tests` "studio import" copies an `.obj` with its `.mtl` and texture (31 assertions). `tools/deps/victus_deps.py check` and `osv` report no problems for ufbx 0.23.1.
- **Consequences:** No Studio UI for model import settings yet (`.meta` only, as for glTF); separate roughness and metalness textures are not packed into one (factors are used, with a warning); bump/height maps are not converted to normal maps; layered and procedural textures, NURBS and subdivision surfaces beyond ufbx's tessellation, constraints and FBX-specific material graphs are not imported; animated light, camera and material properties are dropped; only the first skin deformer per mesh is used; FBX files that reference textures with absolute paths need the textures next to the model. The importer key in `.meta` remains `gltf` for all model formats.

### ADR-0174: Asset Store providers and licence-checked installs (`engine/library`)

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** The owner asked for a unified Asset Store inside VICTUS Studio with search, previews, licence information and one-click installation from Poly Haven, ambientCG, Kenney, Smithsonian Open Access, Sketchfab, BigSoundBank and the National Park Service, respecting each provider's licence, attribution, authentication and redistribution terms. Unity (Asset Store window, Package Manager "My Assets"), Unreal (Fab inside the editor, Quixel Bridge) and Godot (AssetLib tab) all browse remote catalogues in the editor and copy into the project; Blender's Poly Haven and BlenderKit add-ons download per resolution and build materials. Terms checked on 2026-11-21: Poly Haven assets CC0 (https://polyhaven.com/license) and its public API asks for a unique User-Agent, a visible "Poly Haven" credit and no implied endorsement (https://github.com/Poly-Haven/Public-API/blob/master/ToS.md); ambientCG CC0 (https://docs.ambientcg.com/license/, API https://ambientcg.com/api/v2/full_json); Kenney CC0 (https://kenney.nl/support) but downloads are signed links behind a donation dialog, so no direct API; Smithsonian 3D API files are released under Open Access CC0 (https://3d-api.si.edu/api-docs/, https://www.si.edu/openaccess/faq), with trademarks and privacy excluded; Sketchfab Data API v3 (https://sketchfab.com/developers/data-api/v3) needs the user's own token for downloads and models carry per-model CC licences; BigSoundBank CC0/WTFPL (https://bigsoundbank.com/droit.html); the NPS Sound Gallery is U.S. government work unless a page credits someone else (https://www.nps.gov/subjects/sound/gallery.htm, https://www.nps.gov/aboutus/disclaimer.htm).
- **Decision:** New module `engine/library` (`VICTUS::Library`, depends on Assets): a `Provider` interface (`search`, `plan`) with seven implementations, an `HttpClient` that runs the system `curl` program through `victus::process` (no new linked dependency; `--proto =https` for requests and redirects, unique User-Agent `VICTUS-Studio/<version> (+repo)`; Windows 10+ and macOS ship curl, Linux distributions package it) and a `CachingHttpClient` that keeps API responses and previews in the user data dir only (never in the project). Every asset carries a `Licence` (SPDX id, URL, attribution, commercial use, modification, share-alike, redistribute-in-game); `install` refuses anything that is not commercial + modifiable + redistributable in a game, so CC BY-NC/ND and share-alike models are not offered (Sketchfab results are filtered to CC0 and CC BY). Installation is two-phase: `stage()` downloads into `<project>/.victus/library_staging/` (https only, sanitised relative paths, size caps, MD5 check where the provider publishes it, cancellable, ZIP entries filtered by suffix, `__MACOSX`/dotfiles dropped, deflate through `zip::read` with an inflate callback), then `commit()` moves the folder to `assets/library/<provider>/<slug>` atomically (previous install kept until the move succeeds), refreshes the `AssetDatabase`, sets texture import settings (normal/ORM/height linear, compression `auto`, alpha-coverage mips for masked colour) and writes a `.vmat` for material sets: DirectX normals are flipped to OpenGL, separate AO/roughness/metal are packed into one ORM texture (glTF/ADR-0139 channel order) unless the provider ships a packed one (Poly Haven `arm`), opacity merges into base-colour alpha with alpha test. Each install writes `provenance.json` (asset, variant, source URLs, files, time) and `LICENSE.txt`; `write_credits` regenerates `CREDITS.md` at the project root grouped by provider, marking CC BY entries "attribution required", and `victus export` copies it next to the game. Kenney is browse-only: Studio opens the asset page and `import_archive` imports the ZIP the user downloaded (images, audio, fonts and one model format, glb > gltf > fbx > obj). NPS pages whose credit is not the NPS are refused. CLI: `victus library providers|search|install|import-zip|list|remove|credits`; Sketchfab reads `VICTUS_SKETCHFAB_TOKEN`.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Mirroring provider content into a VICTUS-hosted pack: simplest offline story, but redistribution and attribution terms differ per provider (Sketchfab models may not be re-hosted) and the catalogue would go stale; deferred to a curated CC0 starter pack (handoff brief Slice 2). (2) Reading provider HTML everywhere: fragile; used only where no API exists (BigSoundBank, NPS, Kenney listing), with fixture tests. (3) Bundling a separate download manager per provider in Studio: duplicates the import pipeline; all providers go through one stage/commit path into the existing `AssetDatabase`.
- **Evidence:** `victus_library_tests` 21 cases / 284 assertions (fixture HTTP, no network): provider parsing for all seven, licence gate, MD5 RFC 1321 vectors, staging isolation, ORM packing, DX-normal flip, opacity merge, `.vmat` contents, Kenney ZIP filter, CREDITS.md, uninstall. `victus_export_tests` checks CREDITS.md ships with the export. Live install into a new project on 2026-11-21: Poly Haven `rock_face_02` (1k glTF + 3 maps), ambientCG `Wood096` (1K-JPG, 5 files incl. packed ORM and `.vmat`), Smithsonian side chair (GLB), BigSoundBank #3211 (Ogg), NPS American Robin (MP3); `victus import` 0 failed and `victus check` 0 problems.
- **Consequences:** Online only (results cached for 12 h); no offline starter pack yet. Height/displacement maps are installed but `.vmat` has no height slot. ambientCG HDRIs are EXR-only and Poly Haven HDRIs are offered as `.hdr`; Draco-compressed Smithsonian models are skipped (no Draco decoder). Kenney cannot be one-click. Sketchfab needs the user's own account token and only lists models that are downloadable, not age-restricted, CC0 or CC BY. HTML-backed providers can break when the sites change; failures are reported, never silently degraded.
- **Studio window (2026-11-21):** `editor::AssetStore` (`editor/src/asset_store.cpp`) is a dockable panel (Window > Content > Asset Store, Assets > Asset Store..., `victus_editor --show asset-store`) docked as a tab next to the Viewport, like Unity's Asset Store and Godot's AssetLib tabs. Left: the sources with their licence in plain words and "In this project (N)" (installed assets with licence, credit flag, Show/Remove). Centre: search box (searches half a second after typing stops, or on Enter), type filter, provider credit line (Poly Haven's required credit) and Website link, the Sketchfab token card when a token is needed (saved in `<user data>/asset_store.json`, owner-only permissions, never in the project), and a thumbnail grid (licence colour, installed check, double-click to add, Show more for the next page). Right: details with a large preview, a licence card (commercial use / change / ship-in-game checks, credit-required warning, licence and asset page links), the variant (resolution/format) picker, Add to Project with progress and Cancel, then Place in Scene, Use on Selected Object (materials) and Show in Assets; Kenney shows "Get it on Kenney" plus Import Downloaded ZIP. Network, decoding and staging run on store-owned worker threads (one for searches/installs, three for previews, bounded preview cache); the UI thread uploads thumbnails and runs `library::commit` + `AssetDatabase::import_all` in `Editor::commit_store_install`, so installs keep working with the window closed. Non-installable licences are refused before anything downloads. Evidence: `victus_editor_tests` "asset store:" cases (offline fixture provider: search, debounced query, thumbnail decode/failure, licence refusal, install + import + CREDITS.md, already-installed, list, remove, private token file); live screenshots against the real providers in `docs/images/studio_asset_store.jpg`.

### ADR-0175: Asset references, reference-safe rename/move and delete

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Studio had no way to see what uses an asset, and no rename, move or delete: creators edited the folder by hand and found broken references at play time. Unity's "Find References In Scene"/Search `ref:` and Unreal's Reference Viewer and "Fix up redirectors" show users before deleting and keep references working across renames; Godot's FileSystem dock rewrites dependent resources when a file moves. VICTUS references assets two ways: by stable UUID (scenes, prefabs, `.vmat`, sheets, tile sets, animation controllers) and by assets-relative path (Script components, sounds, Luau string literals).
- **Decision:** `assets::ReferenceIndex` (`engine/assets/src/references.cpp`) scans every text file of the project (scenes/, assets/, `victus.project`; binary media, models, archives and `.meta` are skipped, files over 16 MiB or containing NUL bytes too) for 8-4-4-4-12 UUIDs that name a database record and for quoted strings (`"..."`, `'...'`, optional `assets/` prefix) equal to an asset path. `users(uuid)` and `dependencies(file)` answer both directions; an asset never lists itself; entity UUIDs are ignored because they are not asset records. `rename_asset` validates the new path (inside assets/, no `..`, hidden names or special characters, same extension, target free; `.gltf`/`.obj` may only be renamed within their folder because they name companions relatively), moves the source with its `.meta` (UUID kept, cooked data stays valid), rewrites the quoted path in every file that named it by path, then refreshes the database. `delete_asset` removes source, `.meta` and cooked entry. Studio: every Assets row gets Find References, Rename or Move..., Delete..., Copy Path and Copy ID. The References window lists "Used by" (file, by ID / by name, Show opens a scene or reveals an asset) and "Uses" (click through). Rename saves the open scene first and reloads it when the rename rewrote it, so the in-memory scene never writes the old path back; Delete lists the files that will show the asset as missing before confirming. Both are refused during play.
- **Alternatives:** (1) A persistent dependency database updated on save: faster for huge projects, but every writer (scene, prefab, material editor, CLI, external tools) would have to report references; the scan is exact for what is on disk and takes milliseconds for template-sized projects. (2) Parsing each format with its own loader: precise, but every new format needs code; the UUID/path scan covers current and future text formats. (3) Redirector files on rename (Unreal): avoids rewriting files but leaves indirections behind; VICTUS rewrites the few path references directly and UUID references need nothing.
- **Evidence:** `victus_asset_tests` "asset references" (UUID and path users, counts, dependencies, unreferenced assets, rename across folders with `.meta` and path rewrites in a scene and a Luau script with both quote styles, UUID references surviving a rename, refusals for existing/unsafe/hidden/type-changing/companion moves, delete); `victus_editor_tests` "studio asset references" (saved scene with a Script component found as a by-path user, References window, Rename and Delete dialogs drawn, rename rewrites and reloads the scene with the new script path and the same entity, delete removes the record and closes the window).
- **Consequences:** Paths assembled at run time in scripts are invisible to the scan and are not rewritten; Delete cannot be undone (no asset trash yet; added by ADR-0177); the index is rebuilt on demand, so very large projects pay a scan per References/Rename/Delete.

### ADR-0176: Behaviour Tree Editor in Studio

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Behaviour trees (`.vbt`, ADR for Slice 73) could only be written as JSON by hand; the AI Debugger showed a running tree as an indented list. Unreal's Behavior Tree editor (top-down tree, decorators attached to nodes, live highlighting during PIE), Unity's Behavior package (graph with a blackboard pane) and Godot's LimboAI/Beehave editors all edit trees visually with a node palette, per-node details and live debugging. The roadmap listed "no visual BT editor" as the main gap of Gameplay AI.
- **Decision:** A dockable Behaviour Tree Editor window (`editor/src/bt_editor.cpp`) over a window-free model, `editor::BehaviorTreeDocument` (`editor/src/bt_document.cpp`). The document keeps the runtime's own `ai::Node` list in pre-order (node 0 is the root) and offers structural edits that cannot produce an ill-shaped tree: Add Child (refused for leaves and for decorators that already have their child), Wrap In decorator (wrapping the root makes the decorator the new root), Change To (only to types whose child count fits), Move Left/Right, Duplicate and Delete (subtree; the root cannot be deleted). Each edit is one snapshot undo step (200 kept); property edits are one step per widget gesture. Saving goes through new engine functions `ai::tree_to_json` / `ai::value_to_json` (omitting fields left at defaults) and loading through `BehaviorTree::parse`, so the editor and the game agree on every field; the runtime refuses cycles, shared children and dangling indices. The canvas lays the tree out top-down (leaves in columns, parents centred over their children, elbow links), colours nodes by category (composite, decorator, condition, task), shows a plain-words summary ("target is set", "2 s (±0.5)", "hp <= 10") or the node's problem in red, and pans, zooms and frames.
