The palette lists every node type with a one-line description; the details pane edits name, reactive, threshold, count, seconds, deviation, blackboard key, condition (plain words: "is set", "is at least"), value (Yes/No, Number, Text, Position), arrival distance, speed, radius, action name and log message, plus the tree's blackboard defaults. A pill says "Ready to run" or "Will not run" with the first problem. Double-clicking a `.vbt` in Assets, Assets > New Behaviour Tree, the command search and `victus_editor --show ai/x.vbt` open it. During Play, when the selected entity runs the open tree and the tree is saved, nodes on the active path get a status-coloured border and dot and the details pane shows the live status and blackboard; Save during Play calls `AiSystem::reload` so running entities pick up the change.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Reusing the visual-script graph canvas (free node placement, pins and wires): behaviour trees are strictly hierarchical and child order matters, so automatic tree layout with explicit Move Left/Right avoids layouts that misrepresent execution order. (2) Storing editor positions in the `.vbt`: unnecessary with automatic layout and would make hand-written files and diffs noisier. (3) Unreal-style decorators drawn inside their child node: compact, but VICTUS decorators are real nodes in the file; drawing them as nodes keeps the canvas identical to what the runtime executes.
- **Evidence:** `victus_ai_tests` "tree_to_json writes every node field and reads back to the same tree" (all 20 node types and every field round-trip, default fields omitted, cycles/dangling/empty refused); `victus_editor_tests` "behaviour tree document" (pre-order after every edit, refusals, wrap of the root, change type, duplicate/delete of subtrees, property undo/redo, full undo back to the starter tree, save/load, parser error on an invalid file, every catalogued type valid when fresh) and "studio behaviour tree editor" (create from Studio, window drawn, edit and save, reopen from the asset, live debug data during Play matches the document, save during Play reloads the running tree).
- **Consequences:** No drag-and-drop re-parenting yet (Move Left/Right reorders siblings; re-parent by cut is not offered); live statuses are hidden while the open tree has unsaved edits, because node indices may no longer match the running tree; one tree is open at a time.

### ADR-0177: Asset trash and Assets panel shortcuts

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** ADR-0175 added Delete... with a warning that it "cannot be undone". Godot moves deleted files to the operating system's trash, Unity deletes to the OS trash as well, and Unreal keeps deleted packages in source control; a beginner who deletes the wrong texture should be able to get it back with every reference intact. The Assets panel also had no keyboard workflow: F2 (rename) and Delete are standard in all three editors, but in Studio F2 opened Help and Delete deleted the selected entity even while the Assets panel had focus.
- **Decision:** `assets::trash_asset` moves the source and its `.meta` into `<project>/.victus/trash/<unix time>-<uuid prefix>/` with an `entry.json` (original path, UUID, time, size) and drops the cooked data; `list_trash` (newest first), `restore_from_trash` (back to the original path, or a chosen path with the same extension; refused when the path is taken or the UUID is back) and `purge_trash` (one entry or all; ids are validated so `../` cannot escape) complete the API. Because the `.meta` keeps the UUID, every by-ID reference (scenes, prefabs, materials) resolves again after Restore. Studio's Delete now moves to the Trash ("Move to Trash", Enter confirms); Assets > Trash... lists entries with Restore, Delete for good and Empty Trash (with a confirmation). The Assets panel remembers the clicked asset (highlighted); with the panel focused, F2 opens Rename, Delete opens the delete confirmation and Shift+Alt+F opens Find References; the global F2 (Help) and Delete (entities) stand aside meanwhile. The Behaviour Tree Editor gets the same treatment for its Ctrl+S/Z/Y and Delete (it previously also triggered the scene's).
- **Alternatives:** (1) The OS trash: familiar, but three platform APIs (freedesktop Trash spec, `IFileOperation`, `NSFileManager trashItemAtURL`) and no reliable way to list and restore from inside Studio; the project trash is portable and testable. (2) Scene-style undo for deletes: the scene undo stack is per scene and cleared on load, while the trash survives restarts.
- **Evidence:** `victus_asset_tests` "asset trash" (trash keeps `.meta` and UUID, by-ID dependency gone while trashed and back after restore, refusals for taken path, extension change, unsafe ids and missing entries, restore under another name, purge one and all); `victus_editor_tests` "studio asset references" (Delete goes to the Trash, the Trash window draws, Restore brings the script back at its path with the same ID and its scene user, purge empties it).
- **Consequences:** The trash lives in `.victus/` (ignored by git, local to the machine), so it is not shared with collaborators and deleting `.victus/` empties it; trashed assets keep using disk space until purged; companion files of `.gltf`/`.obj` (`.bin`, `.mtl`) are not moved with them.

### ADR-0178: Folder rename/move and drag-to-folder in Assets

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** ADR-0175 renamed one asset at a time and listed "folder rename/move as one operation" as not covered; `.gltf`/`.obj` files could not change folder because their buffers and textures are named relative to it. Unity, Unreal and Godot all let you rename a folder and drag assets onto folders in their content browsers, fixing references as they go.
- **Decision:** `assets::rename_folder(db, from, to)` moves the directory in one filesystem rename (so companions such as `.bin`, `.mtl` and textures beside a `.gltf` travel with it, along with non-asset files), keeps every `.meta` and therefore every UUID, and rewrites quoted path references (`"x"`, `'x'`, with or without `assets/`) to every moved asset in project text files, including files that moved with the folder. It refuses an existing target, a target inside the source, unsafe names and non-folders without changing anything. Studio: right-click a folder in the Assets folder list for Rename or Move Folder... (a dialog showing how many assets move; the open scene is saved first and reloaded if it was rewritten; the folder view follows the move), and dropping an asset on a folder row (or All assets) moves it there via the existing reference-safe rename.
- **Alternatives:** Renaming each asset in turn with `rename_asset`: one reference scan per asset and impossible for `.gltf`/`.obj`, whose companions must move together.
- **Evidence:** `victus_asset_tests` "asset folders" (refusals leave files in place, nested moves, UUIDs kept, scene and moved-script rewrites, an unrelated `audio/hit.wav.bak` string untouched, `.gltf` with its `.bin`); `victus_editor_tests` "studio asset references" (dialog draws, scene rewritten and reloaded with the entity's script path updated, folder view follows, move-into-itself refused, move to root and no-op move).
- **Consequences:** Paths assembled at run time (`"audio/" .. name`) are still not rewritten. Follow-up (same day): the folder list also shows folders that exist only on disk (rescanned every 2 s and after folder changes, hidden names skipped, capped at 4096), Assets > New Folder... and the folder menu's New Folder Inside... create them, and Delete Folder removes a folder only when it holds no files, so a folder delete never needs the Trash.

### ADR-0179: VICTUS character pipeline — Orion and Nova rebuilt as production humanoids from the CC0 MakeHuman base

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21). Supersedes the geometry, rig and texturing parts of ADR-0121; keeps ADR-0069's embedding and template integration.
- **Context:** ADR-0121's starter characters were android mannequins sculpted as signed-distance fields: about 26k triangles, flat material colours with no texture maps, no face rig or blend shapes, sculpted caps for hair, mitten hands without finger joints, 21 joints and 7 clips. Unity and Unreal ship humanoid starter characters with skin and cloth texture sets, finger and twist joints, facial shapes and a large clip library (Unreal's Manny and Quinn share one skeleton; Unity's Starter Assets use the Mecanim humanoid). Rebuilding faces, hands and joint topology by hand in code was not converging: the SDF heads never read as human. A production base mesh with clean edge flow, a reference rig and facial poses is available under CC0 from MakeHuman (assets only; the application code is AGPL).
- **Decision:** A reusable pipeline in `tools/characters` (Python 3.11, NumPy, SciPy, Pillow; `bpy` only for the Cycles AO bake) builds characters from a `Spec` (MakeHuman macro values, skull height, skeleton profile) and a `Style` (skin, lips, iris, hair colour and style, suit and trim colours). Stages: `mh.py` reads the vendored, unmodified MakeHuman assets (`third_party/makehuman`, commit `a8bc2d5`); `fit.py` applies the macro targets, poses the A-pose, collapses the 163-bone reference rig onto a 60-joint VICTUS Humanoid layout whose first 21 joints are the old skeleton in its old order (then twist joints, three joints per finger, Jaw, Eye.L/R) and derives 44 ARKit-named facial blend shapes from the facial pose units; `assemble.py` splits skin and flight suit (suit offset outward with cut-plane cuffs, skin continuing under the suit so posing does not open gaps), builds eyes, teeth/tongue, a scalp hair shell, brow shells, lash cards and style geometry (Nova's bun), and repacks UV islands (head and hands at 2.3× density); `meshops.py` subdivides once with Catmull-Clark expressed as a sparse operator so positions, weights and morphs stay consistent; `materials.py`/`bake.py` evaluate per-texel material functions on the real surface (pores, skin lines, nails, knuckles, stubble, freckles; suit panels, seams, stitching, pads, emissive piping; iris, limbal ring, sclera; strand patterns by line-integral convolution along a hair flow field) into base colour, tangent-space normal (from height), ORM with a Cycles AO bake, emissive and groom alpha; `anim.py` generates 41 clips (the original seven with unchanged names, lengths and phases, plus strafes, sprint, starts/stops, turns, crouch set, actions, grip poses and five facial clips); `gltf.py` writes one self-contained GLB with five materials, byte joints/weights, 16-bit indices and sparse morph deltas quantised with `KHR_mesh_quantization` (which the importer now accepts as a required extension, since every accessor is decoded through cgltf's float readers). Eight sockets follow one frame convention. `cmake/EmbedBinary.cmake` embeds the larger GLBs with the assembler's `.incbin` on GCC/Clang (byte arrays remain for MSVC), exposed as `std::span` accessors. Asset ids, paths, UUIDs, the controller, the node names scripts use (`Rig`, `Orion_Body[0]`, the core joints, existing sockets) are unchanged; `project::kCharacterJointCount` is 60 and `kCharacterCoreJointCount` 21; `StarterCharacter::height` gives each character's own height while templates keep scaling against the nominal 1.70 m so Orion stays taller than Nova. The SDF generator (`anatomy.py`, `mesher.py`, `sdf.py`, `test_sdf.py`) and the unused `.blend` export are deleted rather than kept beside the new pipeline.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Keep sculpting SDF anatomy at higher resolution: rejected after two iterations; resolution was not the problem, facial structure and edge flow were. (2) Hand-model in Blender and commit `.blend` sources: not reproducible by the agent-driven workflow and not a reusable pipeline. (3) Unity/Unreal sample characters, Mixamo or marketplace assets: licence terms forbid redistribution in an engine or are proprietary. (4) MPFB2 (MakeHuman's Blender add-on) for the fitting: GPL code at build time and an interactive workflow; the needed parts (target application, proxy fitting, weight collapse) are small enough to own.
- **Evidence:** `tools/characters/test_pipeline.py` 7/7 (subdivision operator affine and weight-preserving, glTF tangent handedness, quaternion round trip, quantised sparse morph and 255-sum byte weights, 60-joint layout with the original 21 first, MakeHuman loaders, original clip durations). `victus_asset_tests` starter-character cases (see docs/STATUS.md for counts): zero importer warnings, five materials each with base colour, normal and ORM textures, 100k-140k triangles, 44 blend shapes per part, 60 joints, per-character height, the original seven clips with their lengths among 41, all eight sockets under their parents, and the shared controller driving Idle (ankle stays at the model's own rest height), Walk strides, Jump, Fall, Land and Interact on the imported models. Runtime and Blender captures in `docs/images/characters/`.
- **Consequences:** Each GLB is about 15 MB (textures dominate); the engine binary grows by about 30 MB for the two embedded characters. LOD 0 is about 125k triangles; coarser LODs still come from the importer's meshoptimizer pass rather than authored LODs. The engine's PBR shader has no subsurface scattering, hair anisotropy or detail-map layer yet, so skin and hair are lit as standard metal/rough surfaces; there is no equipment library (boots, gloves, armour, backpacks) or attach API beyond named socket nodes, no Studio character workbench, no twist/corrective drivers at runtime (twist joints are skinned but clips do not yet distribute roll into them) and no facial animation layer in the shipped controller. These are the follow-up units on the roadmap.

### ADR-0180: Machine-wide shared cooked-asset cache and compressed-only large texture levels

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Each project cooks its assets into its own `.victus/cache`. The ADR-0179 starter characters made that expensive: every new 3D or multiplayer project re-encoded two sets of sixteen 2048 px maps into BC7 and ASTC (about 25 s on two cores) and stored about 700 MB of cooked data (RGBA8 mips plus both compressed variants), and the test suite, which creates dozens of template projects, filled a 20 GB disk. Unreal keeps a local shared derived-data cache under the user profile and Unity offers the Accelerator for the same reason: cooked results are a pure function of the source bytes, the importer and its settings.
- **Decision:** `assets::shared_cooked_cache_dir()` names a machine-wide cache (`$XDG_CACHE_HOME/victus/cooked` or `~/.cache/victus/cooked`, `~/Library/Caches/VICTUS/cooked`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\VICTUS\Cache\cooked`; disabled on Android). `AssetDatabase::import` looks up `<importer>-v<cooked format>-<source key>.vcooked` (the key already hashes source bytes, importer, importer version and settings) and, when the entry's header carries the same key and no dependency files, hard-links it into the project cache through a temporary name and an atomic rename (a copy across file systems). Fresh imports are stored back when they are self-contained, produced no importer warnings (a cache hit would not repeat them) and are at least 256 KiB. Entries are touched on use and the least recently used go once the cache exceeds `VICTUS_SHARED_CACHE_MB` (default 8192). `VICTUS_SHARED_CACHE` relocates the cache or turns it off. Because projects only ever replace cooked files by rename, a linked file is never modified in place. Separately, `ModelImportSettings::rgba8_max_size` drops the RGBA8 copies of levels larger than the given size when a compressed variant covers them (the cooked format has allowed this since ADR-0149); the runtime decodes them on the CPU for a device with neither codec. Starter-character metas use `texture_compression: auto` and `rgba8_max_size: 256`.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Ship pre-cooked character data inside the engine: ties the engine binary to one cooked format and settings, and still copies hundreds of megabytes into each project. (2) Smaller character textures: gives up the detail ADR-0179 exists for. (3) A network cache server: useful for teams later; the local cache is the prerequisite.
- **Evidence:** `victus_asset_tests -tc="shared cooked*"` (a second project with the same bytes links the entry: hit counted, identical cooked bytes, up to date; changed settings make a second entry; the project copy survives the entry's removal; a zero limit trims everything; `off` disables it) and `-tc="model import settings: rgba8*"` (no stripping without a variant; levels above 256 px empty and decoded from BC7 on demand). Measured: `victus new --template 3d` 24.4 s for the first project, 1.3 s for the second; 264 MB of cooked data in total instead of 696 MB per project.
- **Consequences:** The first project on a machine still pays for encoding. The cache is unbounded below its size cap and has no CLI yet for listing or clearing it (delete the directory). Hard links share one inode between projects, so disk-usage tools count it once.

### ADR-0181: Character validation and socket attachment API

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** ADR-0179 made Orion and Nova the reference humanoids, but nothing told a user whether their own model could replace them: a renamed core joint, a centimetre-scale FBX or a missing Land clip only showed up as a broken template at run time. Sockets sit inside the skeleton (`Socket.Hand.R` under `Hand.R`), and the documented `entity:find_child` only searches direct children, so attaching equipment from Luau did not work as described.
- **Decision:** `project::validate_character(CookedModel, options)` returns a report (joints, sockets, triangles, LODs, clips, morph targets, materials, textures, bind-pose height) and issues with a severity and a code. Errors: no skin; a core joint missing, out of the original 21-joint order or under the wrong parent; a socket missing; a controller clip (Idle, Walk, Run, Jump, Fall, Land, Interact) missing or empty; skin indices out of range; a bind-pose height outside 1.2-2.4 m. Warnings: joint count other than 60, socket under an unexpected parent, unskinned meshes, unnormalised weights, feet off the ground, over 20k triangles without LODs, materials without base colour or normal maps. `victus character-check <model|orion|nova> [--json] [--any-joints] [--strict]` imports a glTF/FBX (textures at 256 px) and prints it, exit 1 on errors. `Scene::find_descendant` (breadth-first, root excluded) and `Scene::attach_to_socket(item, root, socket, keep_world)` back the Luau methods `entity:find_descendant(name)` and `character:attach(item, socket [, keep_world])`, which snaps the item to the socket (identity local position and rotation, scale kept) and returns the socket or nil. Added to script API 1.1, which is still the current, unreleased minor.
- **Alternatives:** (1) A validator in the Python pipeline only: would not see FBX imports or the engine's own conversion. (2) Making `find_child` recursive: changes behaviour existing scripts rely on. (3) A dedicated Equipment component: comes with authored equipment parts; the attach call is what it will use.
- **Evidence:** `victus_asset_tests -tc="character validation*"` (both starters have no errors or warnings; renamed joint, reordered skin joints, missing socket, missing clip and centimetre units are each reported; relaxed options downgrade them), `victus_scene_tests -tc="*attach_to_socket*"`, `victus_script_tests -tc="script: scene, entity and input API"`, `victus character-check orion` and `nova` print OK.
- **Consequences:** The height check reads mesh-local vertex positions and assumes the mesh nodes have no scale of their own. Studio has no validation panel yet; the report structure is what it will show.

### ADR-0182: Twist drivers in animation controllers

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** The 60-joint skeleton (ADR-0179) has forearm twist joints whose skin weights come from MakeHuman, but clips only rotate the hands; with nothing driving the twist joints, rolling the wrist twisted the whole roll into the few vertices next to it. Unreal (Twist Corrective / roll bone nodes in Control Rig and AnimBP) and Maya/Blender rigs solve this with drivers that copy a fraction of a joint's twist to its twist bone.
- **Decision:** `Controller::twists` (`"twist"` in `.vanim`, top level only): `{joint, source, weight}`. At run time, after the layers and before IK, the twist joint's pose is multiplied by `weight` of the swing-twist twist of the source (a sibling: its rotation relative to rest in the shared parent's frame about the sibling's rest offset; the parent: its rotation relative to rest in its own frame about the joint's rest offset; negative weights counter-rotate). Drivers naming joints a model lacks are skipped. The starter controller drives `LowerArmTwist.L/R` at 0.5 from `Hand.L/R`.
- **Alternatives:** (1) Baking twist keys into every clip: breaks for retargeted clips and IK-adjusted hands. (2) Corrective blend shapes: need sculpted shapes per pose; drivers are the first step. (3) Upper-arm and thigh drivers: these twist joints sit at the elbow and knee halves of their segments, which already inherit the full roll, so a counter-rotation would make it worse; left out until the rig gains shoulder- and hip-side twist joints.
- **Evidence:** `victus_animation_tests -tc="twist drivers*"`: a 30 degree bend plus 80 degree roll of the hand turns the twist joint 40 degrees about the forearm and ignores the bend; a parent-sourced driver at -0.25 of a 40 degree roll gives -10 degrees; JSON round-trip and validation messages. `victus_asset_tests -tc="starter characters*"` runs the shared controller with the drivers on both characters.
- **Consequences:** Drivers apply only to controller-driven animators (direct `play_animation` without a controller leaves twist joints at rest). The twist joints stay on the arms' animated set, so a layer cannot override them.

### ADR-0183: Screen-space sun contact shadows in the SSAO pass

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** Cascaded shadow maps spread a few thousand texels over tens of metres, so small-scale sun occlusion (a character's feet on the ground, crates touching, cracks, bolts, grass) is lost or detached from its caster by the depth and normal biases. Unreal Engine offers per-light Contact Shadows that trace from each pixel towards the light through the depth buffer ([Epic](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/contact-shadows-in-unreal-engine)); Bend Studio shipped screen-space sun shadows in Days Gone ([Bend Studio](https://www.bendstudio.com/blog/inside-bend-screen-space-shadows/)). docs/ENGINE_BENCHMARK.md ranks this as the cheapest visible gap against those engines.
- **Decision:** `ssao.frag` gains a second output: green = sun contact shadow. From each pixel's view-space position (lifted along the reconstructed normal) it marches `contact_shadow_steps` taps over `Environment::contact_shadow_length` metres towards the sun (view-space direction in `SsaoUniforms`), with per-pixel interleaved-gradient jitter; a tap is occluded when the depth buffer surface lies in front of it by more than a depth-relative epsilon and less than `contact_shadow_thickness` (scaled up with distance). Hits near the end of the ray and near the screen edges are softened, and the result fades out with the AO fade distance. The SSAO targets become `RG8_UNORM`; `ssao_blur.frag` blurs both channels with the same depth-aware weights; `pbr.frag` multiplies only the sun's direct term by it (`camera_pos.w` = 2), AO stays on ambient. The pass runs when SSAO or contact shadows are active and forces the depth prepass; with SSAO off it skips the AO taps. `RenderSettings::contact_shadows` / `contact_shadow_steps`: off on Low and Medium (like SSAO, because the depth prepass they force is the main cost: on Medium the 3D template went from 15 to 17 passes and 83 to 101 draw calls), 12 / 16 steps on High / Ultra; the render path report lists `contact_shadows`. The gameplay `EnvironmentComponent` exposes `contact_shadow_length` (0 = off, the default, as in Unreal) and `contact_shadow_thickness`; the 3D templates use 0.3 m.
- **Alternatives:** (1) A separate full-resolution pass (Days Gone's compute wavefront): sharper and handles long rays, but adds a pass and a target; sharing the SSAO pass reuses its depth reconstruction and target, and half resolution is acceptable for short rays. (2) Per-light contact shadows for point and spot lights: needs per-light screen-space data or a march in the forward shader for every light; deferred. (3) Higher shadow-map resolution or virtual shadow maps: the real fix for distant detail, but far more work (listed in the benchmark).
- **Evidence:** `victus_gpu_tests` "gpu quality: sun contact shadows catch small occluders the shadow map misses" (shadow maps off; ground behind a 0.3 m cube darkens by 217 of 765 luminance, open ground and the sun side within 12; length 0 removes the pass) and the existing SSAO case unchanged.
- **Consequences:** Only on-screen occluders cast; thin objects can leak light or thick ones over-shadow behind them (thickness is one global guess); blended surfaces read the opaque pixels' value like AO; at half resolution the shadow edge is soft. Local lights get no contact shadows yet.

### ADR-0184: Dithered LOD cross-fade

**Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21)

**Context:** LOD selection (ADR-0049) with hysteresis (ADR-0156) stops levels flickering at a threshold, but each switch still pops in one frame. Shipping engines hide the switch: Unity's LOD Group "Cross Fade" and Unreal's dithered LOD transition both dissolve the old level into the new one with a screen-door pattern over a short interval, which suits a forward renderer with TAA because no sorting or blending is needed. The engine benchmark (`docs/ENGINE_BENCHMARK.md`) ranked it the second gap after contact shadows.

**Decision:** The per-view LOD history now also stores the level a draw is fading from and the fade step. When a tracked draw (one with a motion id) changes level, it fades over `RenderSettings::lod_fade_frames` frames (default 8, clamped to 64, 0 snaps): the incoming level is drawn keeping the pixels whose interleaved-gradient-noise dither is below `step / (frames + 1)` and a copy of the outgoing level keeps the rest, so together they cover each pixel exactly once. The fade value travels in `MaterialUniforms.blend.w`; `pbr.frag` discards against it next to the alpha test. Fading draws batch only with draws of the same fade value, skip the depth pre-pass (their depth comes from the main pass), and the outgoing copy casts no shadow (shadow passes switch at once). Blended, grouped (instanced foliage) and shadow-only draws, and draws without a motion id, keep the snap. `SceneStats::lod_fading` counts the outgoing copies drawn.

**Alternatives:** Alpha-blended fade (needs sorting and breaks depth); geomorphing (needs per-vertex parent positions in every LOD and a vertex-shader path; better for terrain than props); time-based fades (frame-based fades match TAA's history length and stay deterministic in tests).

**Evidence:** `victus_gpu_tests -tc="gpu lods: cross-fade*"`: four fade frames each draw both levels (LOD0 + LOD1 triangles), no pre-pass draws, and at most 40 pixels differ from the solid coarse level by more than 90/255 (no background holes); the fifth frame draws only LOD1, matching the snapped image; untracked draws never fade. See STATUS.

**Consequences:** During a fade a draw costs both levels' triangles and one extra draw call for the outgoing copy; fades last at most `lod_fade_frames` frames per switch, and hysteresis keeps switches rare. Fading draws lose early-Z from the pre-pass for those frames. Instanced foliage groups still snap.

### ADR-0185: Modular starter-character equipment as weight-sharing shells

- **Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21).
- **Context:** The character brief asks for modular boots, gloves, belt and backpack with no clipping, toggleable per item, on both starter characters, made by the reusable pipeline (tools/characters, ADR-0179) rather than as one-off meshes. Unreal Engine's modular characters attach separate skeletal meshes that follow a leader pose ([Epic](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/working-with-modular-characters-in-unreal-engine)); Unity uses several SkinnedMeshRenderers on one bone hierarchy. Hand-modelled clothing is usually kept clipping-free by sharing (transferring) the body's skin weights.
- **Decision:** `tools/characters/gear.py` builds every item from the surface it covers. Gloves and the belt are shells cut out of the skin or suit along planes, pushed out along rest normals (1.8 mm, 5 mm) with rolled lips, copying the covered vertices' weights one to one. Boots are the fold-free radial graph of a union of two dilated convex hulls (foot; lower leg). That surface is star-shaped about a point in the ankle, so it holds every toe by construction. Each boot vertex takes the weights of its nearest suit point. The backpack is a rounded box bound to Chest, tilted along the back profile with a padded front that conforms to it, with suit-grown shoulder straps. All items use one `Gear` material (2048² baked PBR maps: leather, rubber tread, webbing, a metallic buckle, ripstop). The exporter writes one skinned node per item (`<Name>_Boots`, `_Gloves`, `_Belt`, `_Backpack`) on the body's skin, after the body mesh, so the existing mesh, material and entity indices are unchanged. The covered surfaces are kept, and the body's AO bake ignores the gear so that hiding an item leaves no holes or dark patches. The build refuses to write a character whose rest-pose LOD 0 has a covered point within 0.2 mm of the outside of its item, or a back closer than 2 mm to the pack, using closest-triangle signed distances. The same change removes the chest piping from the suit's emissive map (it is now a tape) and halves the emissive strength to 2, because the lit chest V read as a decal at gameplay distance.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Separate equipment GLBs attached through sockets: right for rigid props (weapons), but deforming items would need their own skin, and rest-pose alignment with each body would be duplicated.
  - Deleting the covered surface: saves triangles but breaks hiding an item.
  - Sculpted boots: not reproducible from the pipeline, and they would have to be redone per body.
  - Laplacian shrink-wrapping the toe sock: it folded between the toes, and its clearance could not be checked.
- **Evidence:**
  - Both builds print their clearance margins: Orion boots 1.18 mm, gloves 1.10 mm, belt 2.62 mm, back to pack 7.85 mm; Nova 1.24 / 1.03 / 3.52 / 11.25 mm.
  - `victus_asset_tests -tc="starter characters*"`: six materials, the four equipment nodes on skin 0 with Gear primitives and no morphs, 44 morphs on every body primitive, body 100k-140k and total 170k-230k triangles.
  - `victus character-check orion|nova`: OK.
  - Blender renders and VICTUS runtime screenshots are in docs/images/characters.
- **Consequences:**
  - LOD 0 grows from about 125k to about 205k triangles and from five to nine skinned draws when all items are visible. The GLBs grow by about 3 MB.
  - The clearance gate is numerical only in the rest pose. In motion the shells inherit the covered surface's weights, but the rigid pack and the boots' toe box are judged from gameplay renders.
  - Items attached at `Socket.Back` now sit over the pack unless it is hidden.
### ADR-0186: Heterogeneous volumetric fog and sun self-shadowing

**Status:** Accepted (2026-11-21)

**Context:** The froxel fog (ADR-0098) had uniform density inside height fog and fog volumes and was lit by the sun through the shadow map only, so a thick bank of fog never shaded itself and every volume looked like a clean box. Frostbite's physically based volumetrics (Hillaire 2015) and RDR2's fog and cloud system (Bauer, SIGGRAPH 2019) both rely on density noise and on the medium's own transmittance towards the light; `docs/ENGINE_BENCHMARK.md` listed this as gap 3.

**Decision:** `fog_inject.comp` evaluates the medium through one `media_at()` function (height fog plus volumes) whose density is multiplied by two octaves of hashed value noise (`VolumetricFog::noise`, `noise_scale`, drifting by `wind` over the shader clock; the multiplier `mix(1, 2·smoothstep(0.3, 0.7, n), amount)` keeps the mean near 1). The sun term of each froxel is multiplied by `exp(-strength · Σ σt · ds)` from `RenderSettings::volumetric_fog_shadow_steps` samples of the same function towards the sun over `self_shadow_distance` metres (Medium 2, High 4, Ultra 6; Low has no fog), offset by the slice jitter so temporal filtering integrates them. Both default off in `VolumetricFog` so existing scenes and reference images keep their look; the Environment component exposes `volumetric_noise`, `volumetric_noise_scale`, `volumetric_wind` and `volumetric_self_shadow`. The render path report adds "noise" and "self-shadow N steps" to the volumetric fog detail.

**Alternatives:** A baked 3D noise texture (needs a new binding and asset; hashed value noise costs ALU only, which suits the small froxel grid); a separate sun-transmittance volume like UE's volumetric shadow (better for many froxels, but another pass and buffer); shadowing local lights by the fog too (left for later: cost scales with lights per froxel).

**Evidence:** `victus_gpu_tests -tc="gpu volumetric fog*"`: 10 cases. Under a 0.5/m layer 4 m thick the sunlit fog falls below 0.6× the open side with self-shadowing and is equal without; zero steps matches off. Noise at amount 1 raises the pixel standard deviation from 2.8 to 20.9 with the mean 165 → 154 (within 30%); one second of wind changes over 500 pixels while uniform fog is bit-identical. See STATUS.

**Consequences:** Self-shadowing costs `steps` extra density evaluations per froxel (each looping over the active fog volumes plus 16 hashes per octave with noise on); there is no shadowing of ambient or local lights by the fog, and the march length is fixed rather than adapted to the medium.

### ADR-0187: Per-material responsive flag for blended meshes

**Status:** Accepted (2026-11-22). Extends ADR-0143 (reactive mask); ROADMAP "ASR/FSR2 ideas" item.

**Context:** Blended meshes write no motion vectors, like particles, but ADR-0143 left them out of the reactive mask, so moving glass, holograms or additive force fields lag and smear under TAA and temporal upscaling. Unreal exposes this per material as Responsive AA ([UMaterial::bEnableResponsiveAA](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/UMaterial?application_version=5.8)); FSR2 recommends material-written masks with values near 0.9 ([FSR2 README](https://github.com/GPUOpen-Effects/FidelityFX-FSR2/blob/master/README.md)). Making every blended mesh reactive would trade stability for static glass, which gains nothing.

**Decision:** `MaterialAsset::responsive` (0..1, default 0, inheritable `MaterialField::Responsive`, omitted from `.vmat` at 0) flows into `render::Material::responsive`. When TAA and the mask are on and a blended draw has it above 0, a `reactive_meshes` pass after all blended geometry redraws those batches with the forward vertex variant (static, skinned or lightmapped) and `reactive_mesh.frag` into the R8 mask with alpha blending: value `responsive`, coverage = base alpha times vertex alpha (or the base alpha for coverage-mask materials), additive surfaces by luminance. The pass has no depth attachment and tests against the sampled opaque depth, so it works with MSAA scene targets. The resolve then applies the existing `reactive_max` clamp. Studio's material panel gains the missing Blend combo, Responsive (blended only) and Coverage Response sliders.

**Alternatives:** A second colour target on every blend pipeline (as particles do): doubles the blend pipeline set and the surface-shader cache for a flag few materials use. Auto-generated masks from the pre/post-translucency colour difference (FSR2's optional helper): another full-screen pass and false positives on static glass. Motion vectors for blended meshes: they would overwrite the background's motion behind them, which is what the resolve needs where the mesh is mostly transparent.

**Evidence:** `victus_gpu_tests -tc="*reactive*,*responsive*"`: 6 cases pass. A 90% opaque glass cube sweeping over 3-pixel stripes: footprint error against a history-free reference 532,610 at responsive 0 and 270,958 at 1; `SceneStats::reactive_mesh_draws` counts 1 then 0; at responsive 0 frames are byte-identical to the mask off. `victus_asset_tests -tc="*responsive*"`: round trip, clamp, default omission, inheritance.

**Consequences:** One extra draw per responsive blended batch (vertex work only, a trivial fragment). Materials with a custom vertex stage are skipped (the mask pass uses the built-in vertex functions); surface shaders cannot vary the value per pixel; lavapipe only.

### ADR-0188: Low tier switches to TAAU below 0.65 render scale

**Status:** Accepted (2026-11-22). Closes the ROADMAP "ASR/FSR2 ideas" item ("TAAU on Low, measured"); follows ADR-0055, ADR-0141 and ADR-0152.

**Context:** Low renders at 0.75 scale with 4x MSAA and FXAA and no TAA; dynamic resolution can take it down to 0.5. Mobile engines differ here: Arm ASR and FSR2 target upscaling from low resolutions on phones, while tilers make MSAA cheap. ADR-0141 left the choice open until measured.

**Decision:** Measured with `tools/render/measure_low_aa.py` (docs/audits/LOW_TIER_AA.md): Low at each candidate against Low supersampled, on the four reference scenes. At 0.75 the two tie on mean FLIP (0.0287 vs 0.0289 averaged) and MSAA + FXAA is 5-25x more stable frame to frame, so Low keeps it. At 0.6 and 0.5 TAAU has the lower worst-tile FLIP in every scene and the lower average (0.0296 vs 0.0337, 0.0311 vs 0.0395). New `RenderSettings::taa_below_scale` (0 = never; Low 0.65): with `taa` off and `temporal_upscale` on, TAA and temporal upscaling turn on while `render_scale` is below it and off again at 0.05 above it (hysteresis against dynamic-resolution oscillation). `SceneStats::taa_by_scale` and the path report ("render scale below 0.65: replaces MSAA and FXAA") show it; `victus run --taa off` disables the switch.

**Alternatives:** TAAU always on Low (worse mean FLIP on three of four scenes at 0.75 and much less stable); never (dynamic resolution at 0.5 loses 20% average FLIP and up to 45% worst tile); a lower Low scale with TAAU on (blurrier than 0.75 MSAA on the geometric scenes).

**Evidence:** `victus_gpu_tests -tc="*TAAU*,*temporal upscaling at low*"`: 0.75 keeps MSAA; 0.5 runs TAA with temporal upscaling, history on the next frame, MSAA reported replaced; 0.68 stays on, 0.7 turns it off, 0.68 from above stays off; `temporal_upscale` off or threshold 0 never switch.

**Consequences:** Low frames below 0.65 scale cost the TAA history traffic instead of MSAA, and the first frame after the switch has no history. The threshold comes from lavapipe; phone GPUs (Adreno, Mali, Apple) are not measured and may move it.
